### [non-spec.5.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings in non-spec round 5 — BECAUSE the r3-prefix delta is two test additions (MintProto generation pin, tier-8 held-read partition test), both workable against the tree, and a whole-staging re-trace of credential leases across suspend, release, and resume (including a stale or lease-lost replica's step 4 racing a peer restore) found nothing above the bar — ALTERNATIVES: refiling commit-repeat pacing (barred by Traps); filing per-message latency of the path-2 held read under a Redis partition (performance, not correctness).
FACT: go-redis v9 retries a read timeout on a non-blocking command (retryTimeout set when cmd.readTimeout()==nil), so under the tier-8 blackhole partition each Peek/PeekOldest fails after about 4 x 3 s with default options; redisconn sets no ReadTimeout or MaxRetries, and the fixture's deliver() allows 60 s, so TestHeldReadOutageDoesNotStallDelivery_spec_7_2 passes with defaults — EVIDENCE: pkg/redisconn/redisconn.go:204-223 (no timeout fields), tests/tier8_chaos/stream_failure_chaos_test.go:122-130 (60 s request timeout)
FACT: the Sweeper's evictBinding only drops the registry binding and releases no pod or credential, so a takeover never triggers a stale ReleaseThrough on the old replica — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:248-253
FACT: Binder.Resume always claims an idle pod through connect, so a checkpoint restore after a suspension release cannot land on the released session's still-claimed pod; the cross-replica same-pod ReleaseSlot teardown (UNVERIFIED in Open) needs a snapshotless rebuild, which a completed suspension release (checkpoint at step 2) never leads to — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:128-145
USEFUL [Standing context, Traps **Commit repeat pacing.**]: saved re-filing the unpaced commit repeat.

### [non-spec.5.review-security.1]
FACT: The shipped renewal path has no session-liveness check: `credRenewalWiring.Renew` (cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:162) and `Worker.Tick` (pkg/gateway/credentials/credrenewal/credrenewal.go:257) never consult session state, and `Worker.Forget` has no production caller. The spec's §4.9 renewal step 1 "Check session liveness" (spec/04_system-components.md:1543) was never implemented. SPEC-1g3's lease-held check (CODE-4 item 23) is the first stop condition the tree gets, so do not file SPEC-1g3 as a regression of an implemented control. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/cred_renewal.go:162-200
FACT: `EvictBinding` (cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go:81) only drops the registry entry and the cached stream, and releases no credential lease. A stale replica's eviction therefore cannot revoke the leases a peer's re-adopted pod is using; only `Release`/`ReleaseSlot` (now `ReleaseThrough`) do. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go:81-88
FACT: REST message paths run no PreMessageDelivery interceptor today (the only call site is mcptools_register.go:410), so holding a REST message in the DLQ bypasses no interceptor that ran before. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:410
FACT: The erasure `SessionEraser.DeleteBySession` receives (ctx, tenantID, sessionID) (pkg/gateway/storage/erasure/erasure.go:105), and the messaging coordinator is built on the `RedisConcernSessionData` client (cmd/lenny-gateway/session_messaging.go:95), so CODE-6 item 17's `EraseSession` matches the SPEC-5aa `RedisShard(tenantID, RedisConcernSessionData)` routing.
DECISION: no security findings in round 5 — BECAUSE every security bound the proposal touches (active age, retryCount, SuspendedAt lifetime, lease generation, binding stamp) is gateway-written in Postgres or gateway memory, never pod-reported; off-holder held-pod resume and the lease gate fail closed; credential delivery isolation runs on the checkpoint resume and is tested; the generation-scoped release keeps a peer restore's leases (TestStaleReplicaReleaseKeepsPeerRestoreLeases, tier-7a race, tier-9 fresh-lease test) — ALTERNATIVES: considered filing the renewal-race replacement lease that outlives a podless terminal session, rejected because the proposal already records it as an accepted edge (non-spec Edge cases, **Renewal racing a release by identifier.**) and the OPEN entry already carries it.

### [non-spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: add one case, `TestPrimaryFailoverDeliversToRunningSession_spec_7_2`, to the TEST-1 Tier 8 bullet, injecting a failover (primary proxy closes its connections and refuses new dials) rather than a `blackholeProxy` partition — BECAUSE CODE-6 item 7 claims delivery "through a §12.3 failover", which is a fast-erroring primary; under a partition the classification `ReadPrimary` and `StampSync` hang until the request context ends (Standing context **Admission fail-open window.**: `StampSync` has no timeout), so the reviewer's partition test would fail against the staged design — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's `blackholeProxy` partition (encodes behavior the design does not provide, or forces a new operator-tunable primary-read timeout mechanism); closing the primary `pgxpool` in-process (errors fast but is not a network failover); adding an MCP surface to `newStreamChaosGateway` (larger fixture; REST and MCP share the classification fallback and the `StampSync` fail-open).
FACT: `newStreamChaosGateway` hard-codes `memstore.New()` and wires no tenant store, so a REST request reaches classification with the primary down; in production `requireTenantNotSuspended` reads `s.tenants` first and answers 500 on its error. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier8_chaos/stream_failure_chaos_test.go:89,100; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/quota.go:146-158
FACT: `blackholeProxy` keeps both connections open and drops bytes, so a pgx query through it blocks until its context ends rather than erroring. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier8_chaos/stream_failure_chaos_test.go:387-440
OPEN: under a primary partition (hang, not failover) the message path blocks on `ReadPrimary`/`StampSync` while holding the delivery lock until the request context ends. Whether that needs a bound is a design question for a later round or the owner; the staged design makes no claim about it.
CORRECTS [non-spec.5.fix-design-G2.1 OPEN]: closed by the tier-8 failover case above; the partition variant stays open as stated.

### [non-spec.6.review-performance.1]
DECISION: returned no findings in non-spec round 6. BECAUSE the r5-to-r6 delta has three parts: the CODE-6 item 7 read-pool fallback on a non-`ErrNotFound` `ReadPrimary` error, the `DeliverHeld` read-error-returns-nil clause, and two test additions (the `BindingGeneration` production test and the failing-`Update` case of `TestMessagePathsClassifyPrimaryRow`). None of them adds a store write, a watch, or a store dependency. Under a §12.3 failover the MCP path-2 trace is: fallback row `running`, then a `DeliverHeld` read failure (WARN, nil), then the `StampSync` fail-open, so it delivers as shipped. A stale held row on the fallback goes through `holdMessage`, whose failed re-read answers `inbox_unavailable`, which fails closed and loses nothing. ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing per-message primary cost (two `ReadPrimary` row-lock transactions plus the uncoalesced `StampSync` `UPDATE` per MCP path-2 send, against the §12.3 Tier 3 budget of ~300/s session-state writes and ~1,300/s total). Rejected: OD-A (owner) settled the per-Send write and redesign 6 settled the primary read. (2) Filing hang-versus-fail latency of the primary calls under a blackholed primary (pgconn's default dialer has no connect timeout). Rejected: `StampSync` has the same exposure and it was accepted under OD-A. (3) Filing the delegate_task `DeliverHeld` read failure that leaves a running child's held entries until the next delivery point. Rejected: this is the same class as the accepted **Held read failure.** outcome.
FACT: CODE-4 item 23.2's `LeaseStore.Lookup` returns a scan, KMS, or decode error as an error rather than as not-found, so a Postgres failover makes renewal retry rather than drop leases through `ErrLeaseReleased`. The Traps line "scanLease folds errors into not-found" describes the old scan path. It does not describe `Lookup`. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 23.2
FACT: shipped `Stamper.Stamp` coalesces to at most one async write per second per session with a 5 s persist timeout, while `StampSync` "never skips the write for coalescing". The per-Send primary write rate is therefore new with OD-A. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go:139,174-184,228; non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 5.1
USEFUL [non-spec.5.review-performance.1]: its REST `requireTenantNotSuspended` WATCHOUT saved re-deriving the REST failover baseline.

### [spec-recheck.1.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings — BECAUSE the spec delta since spec-r2-prefix (SPEC-1a step 3e minimal-state carve-out, SPEC-1c Retry policy reduced to a §7.3 pointer, SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** "A replica that acquires the Lease evaluates the condition at once") introduces no recovery path that loses, double-applies, leaks, stampedes, or stalls; the failover bound now has its mechanism (evaluate on acquisition), and a pass overlapping a deposed leader is harmless because the `suspended → resume_pending` write is guarded on `suspended` plus a podless row — ALTERNATIVES: filing the leader re-check's use of a listed row's `SuspendedAt` (read authority is the non-spec Design rule's job, and a stale list only yields a guarded no-op or an early resume).

FACT: credential leases traced across suspend, release, and resume hold under SPEC-1g's generation scope: a podless resume passes the adopter's takeover CAS and the driver's entry increment, so a restore's leases always record more than any earlier binding's stamp, including when a stale releaser's suspension release (clear, migrate, release) overlaps the peer's restore. A late migration from that releaser lands after the new coordinator's delivery point, and SPEC-5i "A DLQ entry held after the coordinating replica last delivered held messages is delivered at the next of those points" plus idle suspension and backlog resume delivers it.
FACT: the driver's claim-retry pacing is a non-spec carrier (CODE-4 item 12, jittered `--resume-driver-backoff-seconds`), so SPEC-1b item 1's "attempts again later" is not an unpaced spec retry — EVIDENCE: review-log-archive.md:421

FACT: SPEC-1g lease generation scope traced across suspension release, a peer's takeover CAS, and the peer's restore entry write; a stale release returns only leases at or below its own stamp, and every restore's leases sit above it. The suspension-release clear write is not generation-conditioned, so a stale replica can still release a held pod a peer adopted; the outcome is a podless `suspended` row either way, with no credential exposure because recycle scrub step 0 purges per-slot credentials. — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:480

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: on a `ReadPrimary` error other than `ErrNotFound`, `DeliverHeld` falls back to the `s.store.Get` row and returns that read's error — BECAUSE staged §7.2 **Delivery of held messages.** exempts only inbox/DLQ read failures, and the MCP path treats a `DeliverHeld` error as an error of the new message's Send, so both reads failing fails the path-2 message instead of letting it overtake held entries — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "call deliverBuffered anyway" (no row to pass; deliverBuffered needs row.ID/TenantID; its "Send fails in streamFor or the admitter" justification is false under SPEC-5f admitter fail-open); return nil after fallback fails (ordering violation); no fallback (breaks path 2 through a §12.3 failover); change DeliverHeld to take the caller's row (delegate_task has no row).
CORRECTS [review-log DECISION "`DeliverHeld` treats its own `ReadPrimary` error as a held read failure (WARN, nil)"]: a session-row read failure is not an inbox/DLQ read failure; returning nil let a new path-2 message overtake readable DLQ entries.
WATCHOUT: the re-read after `sessionidle.ErrSessionSuspended` stays fallback-free; do not extend the read-pool fallback to it — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 7 lead.
WATCHOUT: `TestMessagePathsClassifyPrimaryRow_spec_7_2`'s failing-`Update` case must seed a DLQ entry; without one it passes on the ordering violation.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: `DeliverHeld` on a `ReadPrimary` error other than `ErrNotFound` falls back to the `s.store.Get` (read-pool) row and delivers when that row is `running`; an error of that fallback read is returned, so the MCP path handles it as an error of the new message's `executor.Send` (§7.2 **Delivery of held messages.**: the path-2 message is handled as if its own delivery failed). BECAUSE `deliverBuffered(ctx, row)` needs a real row (`recordDeliveredResponse` uses `row.ID`, `row.TenantID`, and `recordTTFTOnce(row, ...)`), and this mirrors the CODE-6 item 7 classification fallback that already lets a `running` target keep path-2 delivery through a §12.3 failover. ALTERNATIVES: (a) the reviewer's literal fix, call `deliverBuffered` on the error (rejected: no row to pass, and its justification "Send to a non-running row fails in streamFor or the admitter" is false during a failover, where the item 5.5 admitter fails open per SPEC-5f); (b) change `DeliverHeld` to take the caller's classified row (rejected: `delegate_task` has only the `session.State` that `MaterializeDelegatedChild` returns, and it cascades into rule 1 and `TestDeliverHeldReadsPrimary_spec_7_2`); (c) return the `ReadPrimary` error at once (rejected: fails every MCP path-2 message through a primary failover, undoing the settled classification fallback); (d) return nil on the fallback read's failure too (rejected: the new message then overtakes unread-but-held entries, which the spec's exemption covers only for an inbox or DLQ read failure).
FACT: `recordDeliveredResponse` reads row fields beyond IDs (`recordTTFTOnce(row, "response")`). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:309-368.
CORRECTS [review-log DECISION at review-log.md:420 "`DeliverHeld` treats its own `ReadPrimary` error as a held read failure (WARN, nil)"]: a row read failure is not an inbox or DLQ read failure under §7.2 **Delivery of held messages.**; returning nil lets the MCP path-2 message overtake readable DLQ entries. The fallback-then-return-error rule above replaces it.
WATCHOUT: the rule-1 parenthesis in Design **Session-row read authority.** ("the first of which falls back to the read-pool row") counts which reads fall back; it must change with any fallback added or removed. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:17.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.review-performance.1]
DECISION: returned no findings in non-spec-recheck round 1 — BECAUSE the delta since spec-r2-prefix (CODE-6 item 7 read-pool fallback, `DeliverHeld` read-error nil, `handleInterrupt` mutator sentinel, CODE-1/CODE-6 comment and openapi targets, `Run` calls `Pass` at start, tier-8 failover and Redis-partition tests, summary reductions and OD-S1) adds no store write, watch, or per-unit cost beyond what non-spec.6 already weighed, and every failover trace (Postgres failover, Redis reset, coordinator handoff, leader handoff) degrades no worse than shipped — ALTERNATIVES: (1) per-message primary cost: `ReadPrimary` is `Update` with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` then rollback, which takes a row lock (WAL heap-lock record, xid) per call; REST path 2 pays 1 plus `StampSync`, MCP pays 2 plus `StampSync`. Spec gives no message rate, only gateway→pod RPC ~2,000/s at Tier 3 (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:263) against a ~1,600 IOPS sustained primary ceiling (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:115-121). Not filed: `StampSync` per Send is owner-settled (summary Decision 16, OD-A) and is the same order, and non-spec.6 rejected the read cost as settled by redesign 6; a plain primary-pool SELECT would cut the lock cost without changing the rule if a later owner review wants it. (2) Redis outage holding idle-suspended pods (`HeldLen` error returns true, no release): no worse than shipped, which held the idle pod to the 7200 s idle bound.
FACT: the watchdog already issues one `List` per state per tenant per tick (5 s, `DefaultTickInterval`) and `sweepMaxAge` lists every row, so `sweepSuspended`'s listing adds one indexed state query per tenant per tick and podless rows were already listed every tick — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:166, :588-604, :1074-1075
FACT: §16 documents a Tier 4 (100,000 sessions, 2,000/s creation, 5,000 tenants) above Tier 3; the leader re-check at Tier 4 is ~100,000 `HeldLen` calls plus 5,000 tenant listings per 300 s pass (~350 Redis ops/s), within one pass interval at ~1 ms per call — EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:595-598
USEFUL [non-spec.6.review-performance.1]: its rejected-alternatives list (per-message primary cost, blackholed-primary hang) saved re-deriving both and placed them under OD-A and redesign 6.
USEFUL [Settled: Leader capacity.]: bound held at Tier 3; extended above to Tier 4.

### [non-spec-recheck.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: the REST path-2 `deliverBuffered` call reads no session row (it peeks the inbox and DLQ only), while the MCP path-2 call goes through `SessionDelivery.DeliverHeld`, which does its own `ReadPrimary` first. Under a Postgres primary error the two paths therefore diverge. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 6 (Loop bullet), CODE-6 item 7 `SessionDelivery` bullet.
WATCHOUT: the new "a `ReadPrimary` error is logged at WARN and returns nil" in `DeliverHeld` makes the MCP path-2 message overtake readable held DLQ entries during a primary failover. SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** exempts only an inbox or DLQ read failure from the held-before-path-2 order. Filed as a finding; the remedy is to call `deliverBuffered` on a read error rather than return nil (a non-running row then fails in `streamFor` or the admitter and leaves the entry at the head). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:217, spec-changes.md:531.
FACT: `gatewayleader.Gate.Run` re-invokes every job's `Run` on each `OnStartedLeading`, so CODE-6 item 16.3's "`Run` calls `Pass` once when it starts" does give evaluation at once on every Lease acquisition. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go:222-229, :142-147.
FACT: `Binder.Release` releases credentials before the teardown (release.go:66), so a step-4 teardown error after `releasePodCredentials` does not leave the pre-suspension leases renewing.

### [non-spec-recheck.3.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in non-spec-recheck round 3. BECAUSE the diff against non-spec-recheck-r1-prefix is the same four hunks that round 2 reviewed: rule 1 names the `bufferAfterResumedSendFailure` re-read, rule 2 adds `sweepIdle` through `SuspendIdle`, `DeliverHeld` falls back to `s.store.Get`, and the failing-`Update` and fresh-state tests were extended. No staging changed after round 2. A whole-document walk of CODE-4 items 1-24, CODE-5, CODE-6 items 2, 4, 5, 7, and 16, and the Design paragraphs found no non-idempotent replay, unreclaimed resource, or unbounded wait that earlier rounds had not already settled. ALTERNATIVES: filing the adopter `TurnInFlight` gap (see the OPEN below). I rejected it because it is narrow and it improves on shipped behaviour, which expires the session terminally on any replica. Its remedy is also an owner trade-off between holding the pods of adopted idle sessions and suspending a silent turn, and neither choice is a required fix.
CORRECTS [Standing context Open, "UNVERIFIED: whether an adopter's `TurnInFlight` can idle-suspend a turn the dead coordinator started"]: verified. It can, but only for a turn that produces no other qualifying activity. On the sidecar transport, `Attach` returns on `ctx.Done` and does not signal the runtime, so the turn keeps running after the coordinator dies. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:186-187, and the heartbeat comment at attach.go:70-73. In the tree, the only stamps outside the proxy, direct-mode, and await_children paths are the `response`/`response_degraded` publishes. They happen after `Send` returns on the replica that sent the turn. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/events.go:362-369, messages_delivery.go:361,376. Proxy-mode chunks are stamped on whichever replica serves the proxy. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy_usage.go:250-253. A turn that keeps calling the LLM is therefore protected after a handoff. A turn running a long tool with no LLM traffic for `maxClientIdleSeconds` after its admission stamp is not protected: the adopter's `SuspendIdle` sees no cached conn, suspends the session, and the zero hold releases the pod mid-turn.
OPEN (owner): summary.md:47 says the lease gate makes `SuspendIdle` correct. That stops holding after a handoff, because the new lease holder does not hold the turn. One possible remedy is a `TurnInFlight` that reports "unknown" (true) when this replica has no cached conn for an adopted `running` session. Its cost is that every idle session adopted after a rolling update keeps its pod until its next message or until the active-age cap. That trade-off is the owner's to make.
USEFUL [non-spec-recheck.2.review-reliability.1]: its trace of `recordDeliveredResponse` writing no session row settled the failing-`Update` test case without re-deriving it.

DECISION: SPEC-5f close rule restated as "one `response` for each message it wrote on that stream", with one sentence accepting the window where a pre-stream turn's `response` closes a later message's turn early — BECAUSE the stdout `response` frame carries no message id (spec/28 `response` fields; schemas/lenny-adapter.proto AttachResponse carries only envelope_json) and CODE-6 item 5.2 already closes on conn quiescence — ALTERNATIVES: a correlation field on `response` (wire change for every runtime); a skip-first-close on a conn opened over a foreign open turn (new state, pins adopted sessions).
DECISION: fail-open admission (OD-A) keeps delivering; SPEC-5f now states the delivering replica does not suspend while that delivery is in progress, and that other replicas and an earlier committed suspend write do not see it, so the release can end that turn — BECAUSE CODE-6 item 5.3 already applies the TurnInFlight check, and the adopter claim was false for unrecorded admissions — ALTERNATIVES: refuse on store error (contradicts §12.3 continuity); a persisted marker (row unwritable during failover).
WATCHOUT: the idle predicate has one home, SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**; the SPEC-5a and SPEC-5h state-block lines and summary Decision 10 carry no copy of it. Do not reinsert "with no turn in flight" there. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5a/SPEC-5h code lines.

### [spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the turn-close rule is restated as a count ("its stream has received one `response` for each message it wrote"), and the window where a `response` from a turn started before that stream opened is counted for a later message is accepted in one SPEC-5f sentence — BECAUSE `response` carries no message id (spec/28_communication-channels.md `response` fields; schemas/lenny-adapter.proto AttachRequest/AttachResponse), the staged executor already closes on quiescence (CODE-6 item 5.2), and the early close needs a turn silent for maxClientIdleSeconds after a coordinator loss or stream end — ALTERNATIVES: message correlation on CH-ATTACH (wire change for every runtime); fail-safe "first response on a stream opened over an open turn closes nothing" (needs a prior-open return from StampSync plus conn state, and pins an adopted session that gets one more message until the next message or maxSessionAge, the pinning the review log already rejected).
FACT: on one conn the executor serializes turns with a one-slot token, so a second Send waits for the first `response`; finding 1's case (c) (path-4 overlap on one stream) does not occur in the staged code, and the count rule covers it anyway — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/podstream.go acquireTurn/completeTurn (token cap 1), pod.go Send doc comment.
DECISION: the fail-open delivery keeps OD-A's fail-open; SPEC-5f now states the delivering replica's no-suspend-while-delivering condition (the code's `TurnInFlight` check, CODE-6 item 5.3) and the accepted outcome for an adopter or an earlier committed suspension — BECAUSE the condition is a pod-release predicate the code already applies, so it belongs in the spec — ALTERNATIVES: refusing delivery on admission failure (contradicts §12.3 failover continuity and the settled **Admission fail-open.**).
DECISION: state-block labels drop "with no turn in flight" rather than switch to "no turn open" — BECAUSE SPEC-5f is the one home of the predicate and both labels already sit beside or point at §6.2.
DECISION: non-spec Edge label **Turn end no replica received.** is not renamed; its body already names the `CloseTurn` failure — BECAUSE a rename cascades into CODE-6 item 5.5 and summary Decision 16 for no implementor effect.
WATCHOUT: the spec-changes Edge bullet **A runtime that hangs mid-turn.** says the idle clock "never" suspends a hung turn; after the accepted windows it is a pointer to SPEC-5f, not an absolute — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md line ~25.

### [spec.5.review-fresh.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: SPEC-5f's turn-close rule ("the runtime's `response` to the last message it wrote") cannot be evaluated after a delivery path 4 write on the SAME stream, because path 4 writes a second message while a turn is in flight and `response` carries no message id — BECAUSE the earlier turn's `response` then closes the row boundary of the path-4 turn, and idle suspension can release the pod while that turn runs silent past `maxClientIdleSeconds` — ALTERNATIVES: folding it into [spec.5.review-mechanism.1]'s fresh-stream finding (rejected: that finding's evidence covers only fresh streams, so a fix scoped to adopters and post-stream-end replicas leaves path 4 open).
FACT: the shipped executor serializes turns per stream with a token (`attachConn.registerTurn`/`completeTurn`, pkg/gateway/session/executor/podstream.go:151-192), so code cannot express path 4's mid-turn write today; the spec still states it (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:331, spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1602). FACT: the stdout `response` frame schema has `type`, `output`/`text`, `sessionId`, optional `error`, and no message id; the stdin `message` has `id`. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:613-623, 753-766
WATCHOUT: any remedy for turn-close correlation must fail safe (keep the turn open on ambiguity). Closing early is the direction that releases a pod mid-turn; staying open only costs a pod until `maxSessionAge`.

So whether a held-pod resume after an interrupt can ever be idle-suspended depends on runtime behaviour the spec does not state. Two other lenses in this round recorded the same thing. Not filed.

### [spec.5.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: no frame on CH-ATTACH correlates a `response` with the message it answers. `AttachRequest` carries session_id, an opaque envelope, and the generation; `AttachResponse` carries only the opaque envelope (schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:1029-1060). The gateway reader completes whatever turn is registered on the first `response` frame it reads (pkg/gateway/session/executor/podstream.go:306-316). The adapter fan-out sends a frame to every subscriber present when the frame is read (pkg/adapter/linefanout/linefanout.go). — EVIDENCE: as cited
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5f's close rule ("once it has received the runtime's `response` to the last message it wrote") cannot be evaluated on a fresh stream. Two cases reach a fresh stream: an adopter, and a coordinator after a stream end. If such a replica writes a message while an earlier turn is still running, the earlier turn's `response` closes the replica's own boundary. The runtime then runs that replica's turn with `turn_open` false. Filed as a finding. The remedy belongs in SPEC-5f: either accept and state the window, or add correlation to the frames, which widens the proposal. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f; non-spec CODE-6 item 5.2 **Turn close.**
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5a and SPEC-5h still label the idle edge "no turn in flight", while SPEC-5f now keys the suspension on "no turn open on the session row". These differ in both directions: an unrecorded fail-open admission is in flight but not open, and a turn whose `response` was lost is open but not in flight. Filed as predicate drift. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5a, SPEC-5h, SPEC-5f
FACT: the Standing context lists no UNVERIFIED items in this round (compaction closed all eight). The redesign-7 apply log has one UNVERIFIED, the migration number 0184, and that item is non-spec.

### [spec.5.review-performance.1]
FACT: Shipped `sessionidle.Stamper` coalesces `last_agent_activity_at` to at most one write per second per session (pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go:136-139), so redesign 7's `StampSync` (one per delivery, records `lastWrite`) and `CloseTurn` (one per turn, also the turn's agent_output stamp) add at most about one uncoalesced session-row write per turn over shipped. No Tier 3 budget breach is derivable: §12.3's ~300/s session-state row (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:66) already undercounts the shipped 1/s stamping. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:62-72
FACT: In `running`, only the coordinator's Attach stream carries `tool_use`/`agent_output` stamps. Proxy-mode LLM chunks stamp from any replica (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:268-270). An adopter with no stream therefore sees no activity from a turn that is doing tool work only.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5f's sentence "a replica that adopts the session after its coordinator is lost does not suspend a turn the lost coordinator started" is false for a delivery admitted unrecorded under the §12.3 fail-open in the same paragraph. When the coordinator dies mid-turn, the adopter finds `turn_open` false and `TurnInFlight` false, so it can suspend that turn. Filed as a contradiction. The remedy is to qualify the clause and leave the fail-open alone, which is settled. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**; non-spec CODE-6 item 5.3 and 5.5

### [spec.5.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding in the staged spec edits — BECAUSE every bound the proposal touches is gateway-sourced and durable (SuspendedAt, turn_open/turn_seq, last_agent_activity_at, coordination_generation, lease generation all live on the Postgres row or the shared lease record), the pin read still fails closed on resume (SPEC-3h), off-holder held-pod resume fails closed (SPEC-3j), budget-key expiry stays fail-closed in spec (spec/08:408), and a full user revoke cancels podless suspended rows in SessionStore (spec/11:270) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the admission fail-open (SPEC-5f) as an unrecorded mid-turn suspension after a handoff; rejected, it needs a Postgres failover plus coordinator death plus 900 s of runtime silence, the coordinator itself also checks TurnInFlight (non-spec CODE-6 item 5.3), and it is a reliability window, not a security bound.
FACT: SPEC-1g lease scoping never under-revokes: every lease on a released pod is either the binding attempt's own or recorded at a generation at or below the releaser's bind/adopt stamp, because adoption and restore entry both advance coordination_generation. Over-revocation cases are availability, not security. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1g; spec/04_system-components.md:864 (§4.7.8 terminates the old pod before AssignCredentials on the replacement)
FACT: §29.3 step 6 places PreMessageDelivery "before this delivery" only for the running path; held DLQ delivery (path 6) does not restate it, and running it at acceptance satisfies "before". Not a finding. — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:469-472
UNVERIFIED: whether §12.8 DeleteByUser should also erase messages a user SENT that sit in another user's session DLQ (now held up to 7 days); session_dlq_archive has the same owner-scoped gap pre-0092. Not a security-lens defect; a compliance lens could check.

FACT: In the staged spec text each message-path read-failure outcome is stated once, at the site that owns the read: SPEC-5f (re-route after a refused admission; unreadable or refused again goes to the DLQ), SPEC-5i **Synchronous delivery.** (re-read after a failed send; a failed re-read goes to the DLQ), **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.** (a failed post-enqueue re-read removes the entries and answers `inbox_unavailable`), and **Delivery of held messages.** (an inbox or DLQ read failure is not a failed delivery). DECISION: did not file SPEC-5f's refused-message re-route against **Synchronous delivery.**'s failed-send re-route under (g) — BECAUSE the triggers differ (an admission refusal of a `running` row versus a send failure after the coordinator's `running` write), the non-spec table gives them separate reads, and the outcomes agree — ALTERNATIVES: a reduction to one re-route rule was rejected because a citation would have to bridge two different triggers.
DECISION: did not file the path-6 lead sentence "The session's DLQ ... holds every message to a `suspended` session that is not delivered synchronously" against the **Held messages.** bullet — BECAUSE the lead is a one-sentence summary of the bullets that follow it, and the bullet carries the receipts and ordering.
FACT: The turn boundary (open at admission, closed by the latest admitter on `response` or by a restore's `running` write) is stated only in SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**. **Delivery of held messages.** and the spec-changes Edge cases section point at it. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f, SPEC-5i, Edge cases

### [redesign7.apply.1]

DECISION: Redesign 7 (2026-10-09), adapter-reported turn state for idle suspension, was applied from `scratchpad/redesign/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway-redesign-1.md`. Every edit it lists applied, 1 to 43 with 4a, 20a, 31a, and 38a; each anchor resolved exactly once, and no edit was skipped. It redesigned idle-turn detection: the runtime adapter counts `message` frames written to the runtime less `response` frames read from it per registry entry (new SPEC-5ab, a §4.7 `AttachTurnState` RPC-table row and a §28.5.1 `CH-ATTACH` sentence; new CODE-6 item 18), and the gateway's `turnsQuiet` queries it before the `running → suspended` write (CODE-6 item 5.3) and before the `Idle` release clears the pod (CODE-6 item 4 step 2a). SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**, SPEC-5b, SPEC-5i, summary Decisions 10 and 16, CODE-6 items 5.1, 5.2, 5.3, 5.5, and 8, DOCS-1b, RECORDS-1 item 8, the tests, and the files-touched lists were rewritten to match. BECAUSE: the row turn boundary could not observe a `response` that no `Attach` stream received (`linefanout.Hub` broadcasts only to subscribers present at read time), so after a coordinator's death the adopter either pinned an idle session or suspended a turn mid-flight; the redesign-7 run halted on this.
DECISION: Deleted by the redesign: the `turn_open` and `turn_seq` columns, migration `0184_sessions_turn_boundary`, `TurnOpen` and `TurnSeq` on `sessionstore.Session` and their `pgstore` and `memstore` carriage; `(*Stamper).CloseTurn`, `errTurnCloseSuperseded`, and the sequence return of `StampSync`; `PodExecutor.SetTurnCloser`, both close sites, the `attachConn` fields `sending` and `admittedSeq`, and `PodExecutor.TurnInFlight`; the `SuspendIdle` early return on `TurnOpen`, its `TurnInFlight` check, and the `!TurnOpen` guard term; the CODE-4-commit `TurnOpen = false` rule and the `resumeHeldPod` rule; CODE-6's dependency on 0091 CODE-1's turn token (the 0091 impact row and the non-spec opening paragraph); the SPEC-5f mid-turn windows and close rule; the spec-changes Edge case **A turn that no replica marks closed.**; the non-spec Edge case **Turn end no replica received.**; the tests `TestStampSyncOpensTurn`, `TestCloseTurnGuardedOnSeq`, `TestTurnInFlightCoversWholeSend`, `TestTurnInFlightAbandonedTurn`, `TestTurnCloserFiresOnQuiescence`, `TestSuspendIdleRefusesOpenTurnWithoutConn_spec_6_2`, and `TestSessionTurnBoundaryRoundTrip`, and the closer clause of `TestTurnAdmitterAndBudgetRearmerWiring`; `podstream.go` from the CODE-6 Targets and files-touched list; and the Traps line **`TurnInFlight` gap between entries.**
DECISION: Reconciliation beyond the redesign's own edits: the sweep deleted the summary Non-goals bullet "A turn boundary on the session row, or any gateway-side turn tracker", which edit 13 added, because it defends the new mechanism against the replaced one; the Traps entry below and the Settled **Adopter turn state.** entry carry that guard. The summary Watch-out list gains the fixture requirement the redesign's flags state (tier-4, tier-5, tier-7a, tier-8, and tier-9 fixtures that reach idle suspension need an adapter that answers `AttachTurnState`). The checklist needed only the S5 and S13 text edits the redesign staged: S13 already depends on S5, which lands SPEC-5ab, and its tier list already includes tier 3 for the new contract test; the tier-8 and tier-7a changes stay in TEST-1 at S15.
FACT: The redesign's C1 to C10 corrections were applied as written: `writeSessionEnvelope` has four callers including `emitLocalToolCall`; the handler compares `coordination_generation` against the last fenced generation as `CheckpointBarrier` does, so `AttachTurnStateRequest` enters `fenceReadersExempt` and the tier-3 `fencedMessages` map at field 2, and `operationalFenceSites` rises to 13; `turnStateFor` returns an untyped nil for a nil `Adapter`; the tier-8 kill moves to after the runtime has read the message. EVIDENCE: redesign file, **Conflicts and resolutions**.
FACT: The open review finding "§29.3 path-2 trace omits the SPEC-5f admission and turn-close SessionStore writes" (Ledger, round 5) narrows to the admission write, because no turn-close write remains. No §29.3 edit is staged.
FACT: No tier-10 test is staged; the RPC adds no runtime obligation, and the tier-3 test pins the wire.
OPEN (owner) OD-1 · where the gateway queries the adapter: (a) before the `running → suspended` write and again at the `Idle` release, immediately before the pod is cleared; (b) before the write only, which deletes CODE-6 item 4 step 2a, its test, and the 7-day pod hold, and accepts that the release can end a turn admitted unrecorded during a §12.3 failover; (c) at the release only, which leaves a `suspended` session podful for up to 7 days on a stuck count because SPEC-5g pauses `maxSessionAge` in `suspended`. Default (a), staged. Choosing (b) removes the release-query text the redesign's OD-1 entry lists (edits 4a, 22, 31a, the release clauses of edits 5, 7, 12, 32, 38a, 40, and 42).
OPEN (owner) OD-2 · effect of `interrupt_acknowledged` on the count: (a) no effect, so an interrupted turn stays outstanding until the runtime's `response`, bounded by `maxSessionAge`; (b) reset the count to zero, which adds a decrement site in `runtimeops.go` and an `interruptId`-to-session mapping. Default (a), staged; §28.5.3 requires a `response` to signal completion of every task.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the session-row turn boundary (`turn_open`/`turn_seq`, migration 0184, `CloseTurn`, `SetTurnCloser`, the `sending` counter, `admittedSeq`, and `TurnInFlight`). It lost because the gateway sees only the `response` frames an `Attach` stream receives; after a coordinator's death no replica could close the boundary, so the session was pinned until `maxSessionAge`, and the in-memory `TurnInFlight` was invisible to an adopter. Do not reintroduce a row column, a gateway-side turn tracker, a close site, or an adopter-side close.
WATCHOUT: Count at the hub tap on the runtime output's single reader. A decrement in the `Attach` send loop misses a `response` read while no stream is subscribed, which is the coordinator-death case.
WATCHOUT: Fixtures above tier 1 that reach idle suspension need an adapter that answers `AttachTurnState`; a pre-change adapter image answers `Unimplemented`, and idle suspension then never fires (environment preflight check 1).

### [spec.6.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: close the release check-then-act window by having `ReleaseSuspendedPod` hold the per-session delivery lock from step 2a through step 4 (CODE-6 item 4), with one guarantee clause in SPEC-5f ("hands the session's runtime no message from that answer until the pod is released") — BECAUSE only the coordinating replica hands messages to the runtime and every hand-off there runs under that lock; the lock must cover step 4 because a post-clear stale-row fallback delivery (I2) whose StampSync fails reaches the still-bound executor stream — ALTERNATIVES: adapter refusal mode on AttachTurnState (new wire surface, wedge risk); fail-closed admission (reverses OD-A); lock across 2a and clear only (post-clear window); accept the window (barred by the redesign-8 directive).
WATCHOUT: lock order is now delivery lock outside `slotAccountLocks`, nested only in `ReleaseSuspendedPod` step 4; nothing may take the delivery lock while holding `slotAccountLocks`. The one home is non-spec Design **Per-session delivery lock.** — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Design paragraph; current slotAccountLocks takers pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:207, resume_rebind.go:169.
WATCHOUT: do not move the lock before the step-2 checkpoint; a 60 s checkpoint under it stalls every message to the session.
CORRECTS [Traps **Replaced: migrate before the clear.**]: "the release takes no delivery lock" is false; it now holds the lock from step 2a to step 4. Clear-first still stands (edited in standing context).
DECISION: close the release-migration/terminal-drain race with a post-migration `ReadPrimary` re-read and `drainMessagingOnTerminal` on a terminal row, stated once in SPEC-5z item 11 — BECAUSE the §7.3 terminal drain is the existing notifier — ALTERNATIVES: widening SPEC-5i **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.** to the migration (its failed-re-read removal would delete acknowledged messages with no notice); migrate-before-clear (reverses the settled order).
FACT: SPEC-5z item 11 inserts the re-read sentence after "a `WARN` log entry is emitted." in the §7.2 paragraph after the migration steps, rather than at the paragraph's end, because the paragraph ends with unrelated `awaiting_client_action` archive sentences — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:320.
FACT: `Server.drainMessagingOnTerminal(ctx, sess sessionstore.Session)` and `Coordinator.DrainOnTerminal` exist — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messaging.go:73; pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/coordinator.go:196.
CORRECTS [Traps **Accepted migration windows.**]: the terminal-drain race is closed; only a failed post-migration re-read on a terminal session leaves entries with no reclaimer (edited in standing context).
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: none; no deliverable added, removed, or resequenced (S13 names CODE-6 only).

### [spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the release-time turn window by having `ReleaseSuspendedPod` hold the per-session delivery lock from after its checkpoint (before step 2a) until step 4 returns, and by adding one clause to SPEC-5f ("hands the session's runtime no message from that answer until the pod is released") — BECAUSE only the coordinating replica hands messages to the runtime and every such hand-off on that replica (both message paths, `deliverBuffered`, `completeResume`, `resumeHeldAndDrain`, `handleStart`) already runs under that lock, so holding it closes the gap with no new protocol surface — ALTERNATIVES: an adapter "refuse later message frames" mode on the pre-release `AttachTurnState` (new protocol surface and a resume-time un-refuse); making admission fail closed (reverses the settled OD-A/§12.3 fail-open); accepting the window (barred by the redesign-8 directive).
WATCHOUT: holding the lock only until the step-3 clear (what the reviewer suggested) leaves the window open. After the clear, a coordinator message path whose `ReadPrimary` fails falls back to the stale `running` entry row (I2), its `StampSync` fails during the failover, and `Send` reaches the still-bound executor stream before step 4 releases the binding. The lock must span step 2a through step 4. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Design **Message-path read failures.** I2; CODE-6 item 5.5; CODE-6 item 4 step 4.
FACT: holding the delivery lock while waiting on `slotAccountLocks` in step 4 is deadlock-free only while nothing takes the delivery lock while it holds `slotAccountLocks`. Today `slotAccountLocks` is taken only in `applyFailureFromActive` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:207) and `releaseEarlierBinding` (resume_rebind.go:169), and neither takes a delivery lock under it. `PodExecutor.Release` does not wait on `c.done` (archive FACT, pod.go:257-270). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:199-230, resume_rebind.go:168-178.
CORRECTS [review-log Traps **Replaced: migrate before the clear.**]: the release now holds the delivery lock (from step 2a through step 4), so "the release takes no delivery lock" is false. Clear-first stays as the order.
DECISION: close the migration-versus-terminal-drain race in the migration's own home (SPEC-5z item 11). After a migration at a pod release, the gateway re-reads the session (`ReadPrimary`), runs the §7.3 terminal DLQ drain (`drainMessagingOnTerminal`) when the row is terminal, and leaves the entries in the DLQ when the re-read fails — BECAUSE the SPEC-5i **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.** rule answers a requester and removes entries when the re-read fails. Migrated entries were already answered, so extending that rule to the migration would make a store blip delete acknowledged messages and leave the implementor nothing to "answer" — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's one-clause SPEC-5i scope extension (misapplied answer and removal clauses); migrating before the clear under the new lock and draining when the clear's guard misses on a terminal row (reverses the settled clear-first order for no gain over the re-read).
CORRECTS [review-log Traps **Accepted migration windows.**]: a terminal drain racing the release migration is now reclaimed by SPEC-5z item 11's re-read. Only a crash, and a failed re-read on a session that is already terminal, leave entries without a reclaimer.

### [spec.6.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: non-spec CODE-6 item 4 step 4 now says the binding is either released before the attempt claims or removed by `releaseEarlierBinding` and skipped; the "or the attempt replaced" branch and the `SandboxName`-reuse rationale are deleted and replaced by one sentence: the delivery lock held from step 2a keeps the attempt's `completeResume` (CODE-4 item 5.1) from publishing before step 4 returns — BECAUSE `completeResume` takes the delivery lock at item 5.1 before its `running` write and publishes at 5.4, so on the releasing replica no replacement can be in the registry during step 4 — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the pointer-identity rationale (describes an unreachable interleaving; `releaseReportedBinding` still compares by pointer, so nothing is lost).
DECISION: TEST-1 Tier 7a `TestSuspensionReleaseRacingResumeKeepsNewLeases` slot-pool case rewritten: hold step 4 before it takes `slotAccountLocks` until the attempt's `completeResume` waits on the delivery lock, assert no binding is published while held, then let step 4 run and assert the replacement binding, slot, and leases survive and no `session.pod_released_during_suspension` is emitted. It now discriminates holding the delivery lock through step 4 — BECAUSE the old case ("hold step 4 until completeResume has published") deadlocks against the step-2a lock. Holding before `slotAccountLocks` is load-bearing: holding after it would block the attempt's `releaseEarlierBinding` and the attempt would never reach `completeResume`.
DECISION: Design **Session-row read authority.** rule (1) site list gains "the post-migration re-read of `ReleaseSuspendedPod` step 3 (CODE-6 item 4)".
CORRECTS [Standing context Settled **Suspension release step 4.** "pointer identity discriminates"] and [Traps "Replaced: sandbox-name identity in step 4"]: on the releasing replica the delivery lock (step 2a through step 4) is what keeps a replacement out of the registry; pointer identity remains the helper's comparison but no longer discriminates any reachable interleaving there. It still discriminates in the failure funnel (CODE-4 item 22), which holds no delivery lock.
FACT: citer sweep over "pointer identity", "attempt replaced", "holds step 4", "TestSuspensionReleaseRacing" across the proposal: only non-spec lines for CODE-4 item 22 (funnel, still true), CODE-6 item 4 step 4, and Tier 7a hit; summary.md line on `slotAccountLocks` across `releaseExecutor` stays true. No checklist or spec-changes hit.
UNVERIFIED: whether a resume attempt on a different replica (after a lease takeover) can claim a same-pod slot whose `SlotID` equals the session ID while the old holder's step 4 still releases its binding by `SlotID`; the delivery lock is in-process and does not order that. Reviewer of the next non-spec round should check whether the Design lease gate on step 4 closes it.

### [spec.6.review-fresh.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: SPEC-5f's release-time turn check ("immediately before it clears the pod ... asks the adapter again and keeps the pod while a turn is outstanding") is a check-then-act that the same paragraph's admission fail-open defeats; a delivery handed to the runtime unrecorded after the query and before the clear write is ended by the release, which non-spec Edge cases **Turn state unavailable or never zero.** concedes — BECAUSE the redesign-8 directive bars any mid-turn release window and the remedy (an exclusion on the coordinating replica between the query and the gated write) has to be stated in SPEC-5f — ALTERNATIVES: deleting the fail-open (rejected: Settled **Admission fail-open.**); a per-replica marker (the Traps entry **Admission fail-open window.** forbids it, and that entry predates the directive, so a fixer must reconcile it).
FACT: the same fail-open also defeats the suspension write's guard (`last_agent_activity_at` unchanged), because an unrecorded delivery does not advance it; the release re-query is what catches that case, so only the query-to-clear gap is left. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f; non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 5.3, item 5.5, item 4 steps 2a and 3
DECISION: did not refile the §29.3 path-2 trace omitting the admission write (spec round 5, [spec.5.review-edit-sites.1]) — BECAUSE the trace already omitted the pre-existing `last_agent_activity_at` qualifying-event write for a client message, so leaving out a stamping write follows the trace's existing convention and is not a disagreement with §6.2.

### [spec.6.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: No staged spec text accepts a mid-turn pod release. SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** states the guarantee at the release ("immediately before it clears the pod ... asks the adapter again and keeps the pod while a turn is outstanding"), SPEC-5b step 2 conditions the idle release on it, and SPEC-5ab and SPEC-5i cite it in one clause each. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f, SPEC-5b step 2, SPEC-5ab item 2.
FACT: Interrupt suspension followed by a held-pod resume with no message: no RPC or frame tells a Full-level runtime to leave its safe stop point (the §4.7 RPC table has no resume-from-suspend RPC, and the §28.5.3 CH-RUNTIMEOPS table has no continue frame), and SPEC-5i writes `suspended → running` with no pod RPC. Under OD-2 (a) the interrupted turn's count stays at 1, so idle suspension never fires and `maxSessionAge` bounds the pod. That is the owner's OD-2 consequence as the redesign-7 OD-2 entry states it, so it is not a finding. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:665-686; spec/28_communication-channels.md:1202-1218.
FACT: At Basic and Standard level an interrupt sends the runtime no signal (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1778, spec/04_system-components.md:928-937), so an interrupt suspension's release after `maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` can release a pod whose turn is still running. This behavior predates 0092, and the owner kept interrupt pause-and-decide unchanged (summary Non-goals). SPEC-5b step 2 limits the turn query to idle suspensions. Do not file it as a 0092 mid-turn release.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, Edge cases **Turn state unavailable or never zero.**]: the last two sentences ("An unrecorded delivery (CODE-6 item 5.5) whose message reaches the adapter after the release's query and before step 4 is ended by the release. This window exists only during a §12.3 failover.") accept a mid-turn release window. The redesign-8 directive forbids that. The spec staging does not accept the window. Closing it needs a design decision (the release takes no delivery lock, and admission fail-open is settled), so the non-spec loop or the owner has to settle it. It cannot be closed by editing the sentence alone.
UNVERIFIED: the window above needs a StampSync that blocks across the suspend write and the release checkpoint, fails with a non-suspended error, and is then followed by a successful clear. The non-spec loop should confirm against CODE-6 items 4 and 5.5 whether the coordinator's `ReadPrimary` classification under the delivery lock can precede the suspend write by that margin.

### [spec.6.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in the staged spec edits for round 6. BECAUSE the redesign-8 turn check adds one unary `AttachTurnState` RPC per idle-clock firing on the coordinator and one per `Idle` release attempt, with no Postgres, Redis, or etcd write. The admission write now carries only the activity stamp, so it adds fewer writes than the redesign-7 row boundary. Every new value that bounds the design is durable (Postgres row, shared lease record) or lives in the adapter (the turn count, which survives a coordinator handoff). ALTERNATIVES: filing the failover window at the `Idle` release (rejected: shipped `sweepIdle` wrote `expired` over a turn in flight after 2 h of silence, so the new design degrades no worse, and the mechanism lens owns turn-boundary correctness); filing DLQ Redis memory over 7-day holds (rejected: §12.4 and §17.8 state no Redis memory budget to breach, the DLQ caps at 500 entries per session, and **Refuted: the 7-day suspended population.** covers the population).
FACT: the leader re-check does not resume on a Redis error. `BacklogRechecker.Pass` logs a `HeldLen` error and writes nothing, so a Redis outage cannot cause a mass `suspended → resume_pending` stampede. The coordinator's `ResumeBacklog` returns true on a `HeldLen` error only to skip that tick's release, and it starts no resume. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 2 **`ResumeBacklog`.** and item 16.3
FACT: the watchdog `Tick` is serial on a 5 s ticker (pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:166, :1243), and `SuspendIdle` calls `turnsQuiet` inline from `sweepIdle` under a 5 s deadline (non-spec CODE-6 items 5.3 and 18.5). Each over-idle `running` session whose adapter does not answer therefore stalls every other sweep on that replica for up to 5 s per tick. Only the coordinator queries, so the cost is bounded by the number of non-answering adapters among the over-idle sessions that replica coordinates. This is a code-only concern and is outside the spec loop.
WATCHOUT: for the non-spec performance or reliability lens, run `SuspendIdle`'s adapter query off `Tick`, as `ResumeBacklog` and `ReleaseSuspendedPod` already run their work, or bound the stall. Do not file it against the spec: the §4.7 `AttachTurnState` row's 5 s deadline is correct. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 2 **`ResumeBacklog`.** (its stated reason for using a goroutine) and item 5.3
WATCHOUT: non-spec-changes.md Edge cases **Turn state unavailable or never zero.** accepts that the `Idle` release can end an unrecorded delivery that reaches the adapter after the release's query, during a §12.3 failover. The caller's directive bars any accepted mid-turn release window. This sentence sits in non-spec text, not in the staged spec text, so the non-spec loop owns it. SPEC-5f states only "asks the adapter again and keeps the pod while a turn is outstanding" and accepts no window. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md line beginning "- **Turn state unavailable or never zero.**"
USEFUL [Standing context **Leader capacity.**]: the bound of about 10,000 rows per 300 s at Tier 3 saved re-deriving the leader pass cost.
USEFUL [Standing context **Refuted: the 7-day suspended population.**]: closed the per-replica `sweepSuspended` listing question.

### [spec.6.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no spec-staging finding under the reliability lens in round 6 — BECAUSE the AttachTurnState check fails safe under every crash and restart path I traced: an adapter in coordinator hold answers UNAVAILABLE (pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:52-58) and exits hold only by fence or by terminating (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:57-58); an adopter fences before it queries (SPEC-2b step 2, bound pod); a non-answer keeps the session `running` (maxSessionAge) or keeps the pod (the suspended lifetime); a crash between the SPEC-5b step-2 clear and the release leaves a claim the WarmPoolController drains, because no session's pod_assignment names it (spec/04_system-components.md:516) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the unstated retry after a refused Idle release in SPEC-5b step 2; rejected, because SPEC-5f "keeps the pod while a turn is outstanding" already makes the release repeat, and CODE-6 item 4 step 2a repeats it at every tick.
FACT: the Idle release's "ask again" (SPEC-5f) and its clear write are not atomic. Only an unrecorded fail-open delivery (SPEC-5f admission failure plus a failed classification ReadPrimary) can land between them, and both run on the coordinating replica. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 step 2a and item 5.5
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, Edge cases bullet **Turn state unavailable or never zero.**]: the bullet says "An unrecorded delivery (CODE-6 item 5.5) whose message reaches the adapter after the release's query and before step 4 is ended by the release. This window exists only during a §12.3 failover." That accepts a mid-turn pod release in normative proposal text, against the redesign-8 directive. The spec staging (SPEC-5f, SPEC-5b step 2) does not accept the window. The non-spec loop must either close the window or delete the acceptance and state the residual where the owner can rule on it. The Traps entry **Admission fail-open window.** forbids a per-replica marker, so closing the window needs a different mechanism.

### [restructure.non-spec-to-constraints]
DECISION: CODE-4 and CODE-6, and the Design paragraphs on the lease gate, the binding check, the delivery lock, the session-row read authority, and the message-path read failures, are restated as invariants (D1 to D5) with the files to touch, the named tests that pin each invariant, and bounded IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE. Item numbers and the sub-labels that tests, edge cases, CODE-3, the summary, and spec-changes cite are unchanged, except those [redesign9.reconcile.1] deletes: CODE-6 item 4 step 2a, item 5 sub-items 1 to 5, and item 18 sub-items 1, 4, and 5, with item 18's remaining sub-items renumbered 1 and 2. The restatement itself changes no behavior and removes no test obligation.
DECISION: the three read-authority rules collapse into one invariant: a read-pool row selects work and never decides a write, a one-shot decision with no write reads the primary, and a declined mutator returns a sentinel. How `ReadPrimary` reaches the primary is an IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE, bounded to write nothing and to hold the row lock for one round trip.
DECISION: the per-replica in-flight records of `DriveResume`, `ResumeBacklog`, and `ReleaseSuspendedPod`, the 60 s retry record, the attempt timer, the Redis commands behind `Peek`, `Ack`, `PeekOldest`, and `Remove`, and the `ListPodlessSuspended` query text are IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE with stated bounds.
DECISION: CODE-4 item 14 states a constraint on the Sweeper renew term and stages no code change.
FACT (moved rationale): today `Binder.Resume` mints no credentials, and `releaseEarlierBinding` revokes the session's leases through `Binder.Release`, so a checkpoint-restored runtime receives an empty credential file. This is why CODE-4 item 20 exists.
FACT (moved rationale): `rollbackBinding`, `Release`, `ReleaseSlot`, and `ReclaimClaimed` revoke every lease of the session, and the slot identifier is the session identifier. A session-wide release on an attempt's failure path could therefore revoke a later attempt's leases. This is why CODE-4 items 5.3, 20.4, and 24 release by identifier or by generation.
FACT (moved rationale): today `sweepResuming`'s mutator returns nil on a state miss, so the store advances `UpdatedAt` and re-anchors a fresh `resume_pending` window, and the sweep counts and notifies a retry that a failure report produced. `retriesRemain` read from a stale list can write `RetryCount` above `maxRetries`. This is why CODE-4 item 15 exists.
FACT (moved rationale): `PodExecutor.Send` returns a context error mid-turn after the runtime has consumed the message. This is why each held entry's send, record, and removal run on the server-lifetime context (CODE-4 item 6).
FACT (moved rationale): `executor.Send` waits for the runtime's reply to each turn, and `Watchdog.Run` calls `Tick` serially, so an inline backlog delivery, release, or idle suspension would stall every other sweep on the replica (CODE-6 items 2 and 4).
FACT (moved rationale): calling `deliverBuffered` inside `MaterializeDelegatedChild` would deliver a held message ahead of the task input (CODE-4 item 21, CODE-6 item 7).
FACT (moved rationale): an off-holder `deliverBuffered` would fail every `Send` in `streamFor` (`executor/pod.go`, "session %s is not bound to a pod") and strand the held messages of a `running` session. This is why the off-holder held-pod `/resume` fails closed (CODE-4 item 8).
FACT (moved rationale): today `applyFailureFromActive` takes `slotAccountLocks` only for a slot binding and releases it before `releaseReportedBinding`, so a same-replica attempt can claim, commit, and publish a replacement that the funnel's `releaseExecutor` then tears down (CODE-4 item 22).
FACT (moved rationale): today `credRenewalWiring.Renew` re-mints any tracked lease, including one that a suspension release, `ReleaseUncommitted`, `failResume`, or a session-end release returned (CODE-4 item 23).
FACT (moved rationale): a SCAN cursor is valid only on the node that issued it (CODE-6 item 14, `PurgeRoot`).

### [redesign9.reconcile.1]
DECISION: idle suspension is made atomic with delivery by an adapter seal (`SealSession` and `UnsealSession` on `CH-ATTACH`), placed before the checkpoint and before the `running → suspended` write, which also clears the pod; the release tail follows in the same sequence (SPEC-5f, SPEC-5ab, CODE-6 items 4, 5, and 18). Deleted: `AttachTurnState` and `turnsQuiet`, `StampSync` with the turn admitter (`SetTurnAdmitter`, `wireTurnAdmitter`, `ErrSessionSuspended`, `errStampSkipped`), `ReleaseSuspendedPod` step 2a, and the delivery lock held across the release — BECAUSE the admission fail-open let a stale-row delivery during a §12.3 failover start a turn that the release then ended, which the redesign-8 directive bars (DEFERRED lines of [spec.6.review-mechanism.1] and [spec.6.review-reliability.1]), and the adapter is the one component every replica's delivery passes through — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the turn query, the admission write, and the release-held delivery lock as restated constraints (kept the window); a fail-closed admission write (reverses §12.3 failover continuity).
FACT: `writeSessionEnvelope` is the only path a gateway `message` takes to the runtime; its callers are `Attach`'s first frame, `attachRecvLoop`, `SendMessage`, and `emitLocalToolCall` (`tool_result`), while heartbeat, `session_start`, and `session_end` call `WriteEnvelope` directly. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:54, :198, :259, :272; pkg/adapter/session.go:234; pkg/adapter/heartbeat.go:153; pkg/adapter/sessionframes.go:115, :135
FACT: a `FailedPrecondition` stream end is not a reported stream failure, so a seal refusal reaches no failure funnel. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/podstream.go `isReportedStreamFailure`
FACT: the UNVERIFIED cross-replica slot-release race of [spec.6.follow-up-fix.1] is refuted; Settled **Cross-replica slot release.** records the evidence.
CORRECTS [spec.6.follow-up-fix.1] "the delivery lock (step 2a through step 4) is what keeps a replacement out of the registry": step 2a is deleted, and pointer identity in `releaseReportedBinding` under `slotAccountLocks` again discriminates on the releasing replica (CODE-6 item 4 step 4).
DECISION: the tier-8 stale-row test asserts `error` with `inbox_unavailable` for a message sent while the primary is failed over — BECAUSE the seal-refusal re-read and `holdMessage`'s post-enqueue re-read both use `ReadPrimary`, and a failed `holdMessage` re-read removes the entry (CODE-4 item 7) — ALTERNATIVES: asserting a `queued` receipt (contradicts CODE-4 item 7).
OPEN (owner): OD-R9-A and OD-R9-B, in summary `## Open decisions for human to make`.
WATCHOUT: a held-pod `Idle` row is unreachable after this change; the Deferred entry lists the unit fixtures that still seed one.

DECISION: SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** checkpoint-failure/write-miss sentence now ends "the gateway lifts the seal and writes nothing" — BECAUSE the write's own guard (running, same pod, unchanged last_agent_activity_at) misses exactly when interrupt, terminate/DELETE, or a pod failure moved the session out of `running`, so "stays `running`" was false; the resulting state belongs to the transition that caused the miss (§7.2, §15.1) — ALTERNATIVES: enumerating each racing transition's end state (a cause list that drifts); deleting the sentence (loses the checkpoint-failure abort).
DECISION: SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** no longer lists idle-suspension non-completion causes; it points at §6.2 **Idle suspension.** only — BECAUSE SPEC-5f is the one home and the copy omitted the write miss and the unlifted seal — ALTERNATIVES: completing the list (second copy, drifts again).
WATCHOUT: the adapter-refusal clause in SPEC-5f ("writes nothing, and the session stays `running`, where `maxSessionAge` bounds it") carries the same latent claim if a concurrent transition races the seal; left unchanged by design as a separate candidate finding. Do not "fix" it by narrowing to "unless another transition moved it". — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**
FACT: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 step 1 ("The session stays `running`, and the next tick asks again") and the tier-1 test row ("leave the row `running` with its pod", driven by a moved LastAgentActivityAt) stay true: both describe cases where no other transition intervened. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:317, :610

### [spec.10.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed two findings, both on SPEC-5f's outcome statements — (1) SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** restates SPEC-5f's non-completion causes as a list ("adapter refuses or does not answer the seal, or the checkpoint fails") that omits the write miss and the unlifted-seal cases; remedy is deleting the cause list and citing SPEC-5f; (2) SPEC-5f says a write that "misses its condition" leaves the session "`running`", but the write's own state conjunct misses exactly when an interrupt, DELETE/terminate, or pod failure moved the session out of `running` during the checkpoint — BECAUSE round 8 removed the cause list from SPEC-5f for the same drift reason, and the second copy in SPEC-5i was left — ALTERNATIVES: filing the sealed-`running` idle-clock postponement (already examined in [spec.8.review-docs-alignment.1], not filed); filing turn counting for coalesced responses (conformance row spec/15_external-api-surface.md:2069 requires a `response` for every `message`).
FACT: §15.4 conformance "session lifetime" row requires the runtime to answer every `message` with a `response`, which is what makes the adapter's message-minus-response turn count return to zero. EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:2069
FACT: `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/interrupt` admits `running` with no turn check at the gateway, so an interrupt can land during an idle suspension's checkpoint and move the row to `suspended` (pod held) before the idle write. EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:648, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:171-172
WATCHOUT: non-spec CODE-6 item 4 tier-1 test text says "a guard miss ... leave the row `running` with its pod"; if the SPEC-5f "stays `running`" clause is changed, the fixer should propagate to that sentence (the fixture moves `LastAgentActivityAt`, so the assertion holds for that fixture only). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md line beginning "- Tier 1, session server: `SuspendIdle` writes nothing"
USEFUL [spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]: its reasoning (a state predicate cannot go stale where a cause list can) is the remedy for finding 1.

### [spec.10.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in the round-10 spec staging — BECAUSE the only delta (SPEC-5f "A seal the gateway does not lift on a session that is still `running` leaves it sealed") adds no store write, watch, or RPC. Under a Postgres failover the idle write repeats until its guard settles (non-spec CODE-6 item 4 **`SuspendIdle`.** step 3) and a later tick re-seals idempotently (count 0), so a failover seals a session for about its own duration and not for a full `maxClientIdleSeconds`. Checkpoint retries are paced at 60 s by `ReleaseSuspendedPod`'s retry record, the same pace as the shipped §6.2 release step 3 (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:230). — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing "client messages refused by a stale seal reset the idle clock, so the session stays sealed while the client is active" (rejected: summary OD-R9-B already names "bounded by `maxClientIdleSeconds` after the last qualifying event, or by `maxSessionAge`" and is an open owner decision; OD-R9-A also records that the tree stamps no client message); (2) filing per-tick `SealSession` RPCs against a session whose turn stays outstanding (rejected: 0.2 unary RPC/s per such session, no store write, and pacing is code-only); (3) refiling idle checkpoint and pod churn at the 900 s default (rejected: Standing **Not filed** and archive line 4716 cover it).
FACT: shipped §6.2 release step 3 retries a failed suspension checkpoint "on the next evaluation interval (60s)". SPEC-5f leaves its retry pace unstated, and code carries the 60 s pace, so no spec edit is owed. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:230; non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 **`SuspendIdle`.** opening ("last checkpoint failure is under 60 s old")
USEFUL [spec.6.review-performance.1]: its leader re-check Redis-error FACT and its **Leader capacity.** use meant neither had to be re-derived.

### [spec.11.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed two findings on SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** — (1) a client message that reaches the adapter during the seal window is refused and enqueued in the DLQ, and under the staged §6.2 qualifying-event list (unchanged by SPEC-5e: "Any client-originated interaction: a message") it moves `last_agent_activity_at`, so the idle write misses, the seal is lifted, and the session stays `running` with that message in its DLQ and no delivery point until another message or the next idle firing (up to `maxClientIdleSeconds`); summary OD-R9-A's "resumes the session through **Backlog resume.**" holds only under the code's non-stamping (RECORDS-1 7(r)); (2) the adapter-refusal clause still says "the session stays `running`, where `maxSessionAge` bounds it", which an interrupt racing the seal falsifies (interrupt is valid only while a turn runs, which is exactly when the seal is refused) — the same defect round 10 fixed in the sibling write-miss sentence — BECAUSE both make the applied §6.2/§7.2 text wrong — ALTERNATIVES: filing the §9.2 "not evaluated" elicitation wording (rejected: the seal refusal gives the same outcome); filing interrupt-suspended pre-suspension DLQ entries not counted by **Backlog resume.** (rejected: they wait for the client's resume, which matches interrupt intent).
WATCHOUT: any "no message is stranded" argument for the seal window must say whether a client message stamps `last_agent_activity_at`; spec says it does (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:267), code does not (non-spec-changes.md RECORDS-1 7(r)). The write-miss branch is where the two diverge. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f, summary.md:119
USEFUL [spec.10.fix-design-G1.1]: its WATCHOUT pointed straight at the refusal-clause variant.

### [spec.12.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in the round-12 spec staging. BECAUSE the r10-prefix delta has two edits and neither adds a store write, watch, RPC, or lock. SPEC-5f now reads "the gateway lifts the seal and writes nothing" on a checkpoint failure or a write miss, and SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** cites SPEC-5f for the non-completion causes and no longer lists them. Under a Postgres failover the idle write repeats on a store error, and checkpoint retries stay paced by code at 60 s (see [spec.10.review-performance.1] FACT). Under a coordinator handoff the seal lives in the adapter registry entry, so it survives the handoff. A later idle firing re-seals idempotently because no message is written while the session is sealed, which leaves the count at 0. ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing the DLQ wait after a lifted seal (rejected: it is on the refuted list as a latency trade-off); (2) filing UnsealSession failing during a failover (rejected: the "seal the gateway does not lift" sentence covers it, and OD-R9-B owns it); (3) refiling idle-suspend-then-restore churn compared with shipped idle expiry (rejected: Standing **Not filed** covers abandoned-session churn).
USEFUL [spec.10.review-performance.1]: its 60 s checkpoint-pacing FACT and its failover-duration seal bound meant this round only had to check the delta.

### [spec.12.review-plan-conformance.1]
DECISION: no findings under the plan-conformance lens in spec round 12 — BECAUSE the r10-prefix delta only rewords two sentences (SPEC-5f "lifts the seal and writes nothing"; SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** cites §6.2 **Idle suspension.** for non-completion instead of listing causes) and removes no staging of any deliverable of the plan's suspend-and-resume entry (gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:2474-2516) — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the expired-pod recycle (declined, summary.md:76,150 and RECORDS-1 item 9).
USEFUL [spec-recheck.1.review-plan-conformance.1]: its deliverable-to-SPEC map still holds; this round checked only the delta plus an identifier sweep.

### [spec.12.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no findings under the reliability lens in spec round 12 — BECAUSE the r10-to-now delta is two reductions (SPEC-5f drops "the session stays `running`" after a lifted seal; SPEC-5i points at SPEC-5f for "does not complete" instead of restating causes), and every seal failure path I traced is bounded: SealSession refusal or timeout (next tick asks again, or the unlifted-seal sentence); a stale coordinator's seal followed by a fenced unseal (the adopter's idle clock is still elapsed, so its next tick suspends and Backlog resume restores); a crash between the suspend write and the release (orphan-claim GC plus the leader re-check). ALTERNATIVES: filing "misses its condition" as unsafe under an ambiguous commit; rejected because SPEC-5f states no retry, so a spec-only implementor never sees a miss after a landed write, and CODE-6 item 4 step 3 handles the repeat.
FACT: driver pre-claim retry is jittered (`now + backoff + jitter`, jitter in [0, backoff/2), flag `--resume-driver-backoff-seconds` default 10), and idle-suspension checkpoint failures reuse the 60 s retry record, matching §6.2 graceful-release step 3's fixed 60 s. A stampede finding on either needs new evidence. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 3 step 5 and item 12; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:230
WATCHOUT: a message that is refused twice by the seal while the re-read still shows `running` is DLQ'd with no resume write that takes effect. If its enqueue lands after the release's one backlog check, only the leader re-check (300 s) resumes it. The edge case "A held message whose `resume_pending` write does not follow" covers this as an accepted bound. It is not a stranding finding. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f, SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.**
USEFUL [standing context WATCHOUT on SPEC-5f "does not take effect"]: it settles that "misses its condition" means a clean guard miss, and that no store error lifts the seal (non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 step 3).

### [non-spec.8.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 4 `SuspendIdle` step 3 classifies a guard miss that follows a store error by the guard's row in three ways: `suspended`/`Idle`/empty pod counts as landed; `running` naming the pod the guard required did not land, so it unseals as a clean miss does; any other row, and a context end, keep the seal. "No store error lifts the seal." is deleted. BECAUSE a landed `Idle` write clears `PodAssignment`, and step 4 has not yet released the pod (it is still `bound`, so neither Idle-phase nor a reserved hold), so no resume can put that pod back on the row. SPEC-5f "the write misses its condition ... lifts the seal" therefore binds that branch. ALTERNATIVES: keep the seal on every post-error miss and narrow SPEC-5f (rejected: this loop is non-spec, and the `running`+same-pod outcome is known, so keeping the seal is an unnecessary up-to-900 s DLQ detour); unseal on every post-error miss (rejected: a landed-then-resumed row on a fresh pod would lose the seal that keeps the old, still-bound pod's runtime turn-free until the orphan-claim GC reclaims it).
FACT: in the remaining "other miss" rows (an interrupt suspension, a terminal state, or recovery onto another pod) the session is no longer `running` on the sealed pod, so the kept seal is harmless there. Only the context-end case can leave a sealed `running` session, and that is the outcome SPEC-5f's "A seal the gateway does not lift" sentence covers. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 step 3; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go `claimIdle` (Idle phase only) and `rebindReserved`.
FACT: an `UnsealSession` that reaches a newer registry entry is harmless, because a new entry starts unsealed and a fenced newer generation answers `coordinator_handoff_stale`. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md **Session seal.** Handlers.
CORRECTS [review-log WATCHOUTs and USEFUL at the ledger entries quoting "No store error lifts the seal" (round-7 spec WATCHOUT, the r-12 USEFUL, and the "keyed on the write's true outcome" WATCHOUT)]: a post-store-error miss on a `running` row that still names the guarded pod is a known non-landing and lifts the seal. Only the other post-error misses, and a context end, keep it.
WATCHOUT: the discriminating test is the store wrapper that errors WITHOUT applying, after which `LastAgentActivityAt` moves. A plain guard miss with no prior error passes under both readings. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Tests that land with CODE-6, tier-1 session server bullet.
