DECISION: per-session isolation of the seal and of the turn count is pinned by one co-tenant clause in each rule's own test (seal isolation in `TestSealedSessionRefusesMessageWrite_spec_28_5_1`, count isolation in `TestSealRefusedWhileTurnOutstanding_spec_28_5_1`), plus a delivery assertion replacing "the other session keeps running" in TEST-1 Tier 5 — BECAUSE each test already owns one rule, and "keeps running" is satisfied by a row that stays `running` while every message to it lands in the DLQ — ALTERNATIVES: a new `TestSealIsPerSession` case (adds a test name the CODE-6 item 4 "Pinned by" list would then have to carry); putting both co-tenant assertions in one test as the finding suggested (mixes the count rule into the refusal test).

Assert a delivered message (a response), not a state. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 Tier 5 line; CODE-6 item 4 **Refusal.**

### [non-spec.8.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The proposal's only Kubernetes-object writes are gateway `podclaim.DeleteClaim` calls on claim-release failure paths (CODE-3 item 3, CODE-4 item 20.4) and the existing `releaseReportedBinding` claim patches. The gateway already deletes claims today (`ReclaimClaimed`, `drain`), and the claim is `bound` from `Claim` (`writeBoundStatus`), so a DELETE projects `claimed → draining` and never returns an unscrubbed pod to `idle`. No CRD status, finalizer, or webhook surface is touched. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:81,99; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:219; spec/04_system-components.md:409 (occupancy projection, `draining` bullet)
FACT: The podless orphan-claim outcome after a stop between the clear write and the release relies on WPC predicate 1. That predicate keys on the last binding-state transition, which is old for any session that has been running past `claimOrphanTimeout`, together with "no active session references" the pod. So a cleared row is reclaimed on the next 60 s GC tick. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517
FACT: The gateway-leader backlog re-check is off the hot path. A stale leader that overlaps a failover writes only the guarded `enterResumePending`, so a double pass is idempotent. The failover window is LeaseDuration 15 s plus RenewDeadline 10 s, which is 25 s. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go:46-50
DECISION: no Kubernetes-idiom findings this round — BECAUSE the seal is a gRPC to the adapter, not a status inbox; every synchronous path (message, /resume, held-pod resume) touches only Postgres, Redis, and the adapter; and the claim lifecycle edits match §4.6.1 — ALTERNATIVES: considered reporting the millisecond window in which a cleared row and an unreleased bound claim coexist against the WPC GC tick; rejected because K8s optimistic concurrency and an idempotent DeleteClaim make the race benign

### [non-spec.8.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode findings in non-spec round 8 — BECAUSE the r6-prefix delta is only the TEST-1 tier-8 `TestPrimaryFailoverDeliversToRunningSession_spec_7_2` wiring (pgstore with direct read pool, `StampSync` admitter); it adds no store write, watch, or dependency, and the REST path-2 trace under a fast-erroring primary (read-pool `loadMessageTarget`, `ReadPrimary` fallback, `StampSync` fail-open) delivers as the case asserts — ALTERNATIVES: (1) unbounded concurrent suspension-release checkpoints per replica (`ReleaseSuspendedPod` spawns one goroutine per row while `Checkpointer.Sweep` is serial); rejected because the spec states no checkpoint-concurrency budget and the shipped idle expiry already sealed a final snapshot per session, so the rate is not new; (2) per-message Redis peeks before path 2; rejected, two reads per message are small against the §12.4 Tier 3 Redis budget and per-request rate-limit INCRs; (3) a stale ex-coordinator's in-flight release (step 3 guard has no generation conjunct) after a peer takeover; rejected as speculative: the peer's CH-FENCE makes the old replica's step-2 checkpoint fail before step 3.
FACT: `Checkpointer.Sweep` checkpoints the registry snapshot serially and `CheckpointWithTrigger` has no concurrency limiter; the spec names no concurrent-checkpoint budget. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:338-343,374-376
USEFUL [non-spec.6.review-performance.1]: its rejected-alternatives list (per-message primary cost under OD-A, primary hang latency) saved re-deriving both.

### [non-spec.8.review-performance.1]
FACT: The shipped watchdog already lists every session row of every tenant on every replica each 5 s tick: `sweepMaxAge` and `sweepExpiryWarning` call `List` with an empty `ListFilter` (no state filter, terminal rows included), and terminal rows are deleted only by erasure. A larger podless `suspended` population therefore adds nothing to the per-tick listing cost that terminal rows do not already impose, and the new `sweepSuspended` listing is a subset of it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:1074-1081, :1137-1142; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:576-615 (no state filter when `filter.State == ""`), :681/:700/:723 (the only DELETEs)
FACT: The Tier 3 figures bound the idle-ending fraction. With 10,000 concurrent sessions and 200 creations/s, the mean session lifetime is 50 s, so at most ~5.6% of sessions can sit 900 s idle before suspension. That caps idle-suspension checkpoints at ~11/s, or ~1.1 GB/s at 100 MB, within the §17 MinIO 10-12 GB/s budget. — EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:596; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1222
FACT: The §12.4 Redis table implies ~2,000 gateway-to-pod RPCs/s at Tier 3 (one quota INCR per RPC). That is the closest spec figure for the message rate on the path-2 hot path. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:263
UNVERIFIED: `ReadPrimary(ctx, st Store, ...)` takes the `sessionstore.Store` interface. That interface has no primary SELECT, so the D4 example (an `Update` whose mutator returns a sentinel) runs `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` in a transaction on every classification read, one per message. A tuple lock writes WAL and assigns an xid, while the §12.3 Tier 3 primary runs ~1,300 of ~1,600 write IOPS. Not filed: the spec states no message rate, and the IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE allows a lock-free primary SELECT. An implementor or a Tier 3 load test (Phase 13.5) should measure it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:460-463; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:64-71; spec/16_observability.md:521
UNVERIFIED: `BacklogRechecker.Pass` calls `HeldLen` once per podless `suspended` row, one row at a time. N = (abandoned fraction) x 200/s x 604800 s, which is ~1.2M rows at 1%, and the CODE-6 item 16 invariant ("within one re-check interval") holds only if the pass finishes within 300 s. Not filed: N depends on an abandonment rate no spec states (OD-S1 owns that population), and nothing in the staging forbids pipelining.

### [non-spec.8.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed one finding. CODE-6 item 4 **`SuspendIdle`.** step 3 keeps the seal on any miss that follows a store error unless the row is the landed `suspended`/`Idle`/podless form. That includes a `running` row that still names the pod, which means the write did not land. SPEC-5f says the gateway lifts the seal when the write misses — BECAUSE the over-conservative rule keeps an active session sealed until the next idle firing, so every message is held for up to `maxClientIdleSeconds` and then forced through a suspend and restore — ALTERNATIVES: an edit to SPEC-5f that names store errors (rejected because the spec loop converged and the remedy fits in non-spec text).
FACT: the gateway's `PodExecutor` keeps one persistent Attach conn per session, with one turn token, so a batch's messages are written one turn at a time. A sealed refusal ends the conn with FailedPrecondition, which `isReportedStreamFailure` does not report — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/podstream.go:196-208, :330-371
FACT: a sealed session's adapter count is always 0, because the seal requires 0 and a sealed entry increments nothing. So the OD-R9-B summary's "interrupt left a turn outstanding refuses the re-seal" cannot happen while a seal holds. I did not file it because it sits in decision framing, which the bar excludes — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5ab item 2 **Session seal.**; summary.md OD-R9-B **Ground.**
OPEN: the owner should know that OD-R9-B's stated cost of (a), "refuses every message until maxSessionAge", describes an unsealed session with a stuck count, and the seal does not cause it.
USEFUL [Standing context, Traps **Commit repeat pacing.**]: it kept me from filing the unpaced `SuspendIdle` step-3 repeat, a close variant of the refuted item 5.2 repeat.

### [non-spec.8.review-security.2]
DECISION: no security findings for the spec-r10-prefix delta and the whole-proposal pass — BECAUSE the delta changes only wording (SPEC-5f "lifts the seal and writes nothing", SPEC-5i pointer to §6.2 **Idle suspension.**, the OD-R9-A/B summary text, and the S13 dependency on S2), and every control the lens covers still holds: SealSession/UnsealSession are gateway-to-adapter RPCs on the existing link (no pod-to-apiserver path), are generation-fenced like CheckpointBarrier, are kept out of `coordinatorHoldAllowedMethods`, and a nil sealer, a refusal, an Unimplemented adapter, or a 5 s timeout writes nothing (fails closed); the turn count is a runtime-influenced value, but it only bounds the session's own pod hold, which `maxSessionAge` bounds from the gateway-owned row — ALTERNATIVES: filing the runtime-forgeable `response` decrement (rejected: it can only end the runtime's own session's turn; on a concurrent pod the runtime already serves all its slots, and tenant pinning keeps them one tenant).
FACT: `Disposition` on `PodExecutor.Release` only maps to a Terminated condition reason and always drains the pod or releases the slot, so the idle-suspension release (`DispositionCompleted`) does not change pod reuse relative to the failure path — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:257-272.
FACT: the adapter's CoordinatorFence keeps no reset of per-session transient state on a generation gap (only logs `coordinator_generation_gap`), so the seal survives a fence today — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/coordination.go:101-104, :135-146.

SPEC-5ab "Other frames and RPCs for the session are unaffected" currently leaves the seal untouched. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:1943; proposal summary OD-R9-B **Ground.**

### [non-spec.9.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `SuspendIdle` step 3 unseals after a post-store-error guard miss whenever the guard's row names `row.PodAssignment`, whatever its state — BECAUSE a landed Idle write clears the pod and the pod stays bound until step 4, so a row naming the pod proves the write did not land, and SPEC-5f "misses its condition ... lifts the seal" governs — ALTERNATIVES: add an interrupt-`suspended` clause beside `running` (rejected: widens a state enumeration that the next pod-keeping state escapes); unseal on every post-error miss (rejected: a landed row restored `running` on a fresh pod would lose the seal on the old, still-bound pod).
WATCHOUT: classify a post-error miss by the pod on the row, never by the row state. Interrupt keeps the pod, so an interrupt-`suspended` row naming the pod is a write that did not land; a held-pod resume after a kept seal would run the session sealed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_transitions.go `transitionInterrupt`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/interrupt.go Update mutator sets only State, SuspendedAt, SuspendedReason.
FACT: the keep-seal branch's discriminating test is a landed-then-error write whose row then moves to `running` naming another pod; a `resume_pending` variant discriminates nothing further. The in-flight gate's discriminating assertion is the second `SealSession` count, because a repeat seal succeeds. Both live in the tier-1 session-server bullet under `TestSuspendIdleSealsBeforeItWrites_spec_6_2`.

### [non-spec.9.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 4 `SuspendIdle` step 3 keys a post-store-error miss on the write's own effect, not on row state: a guard row that names `row.PodAssignment` proves the write did not land and takes the clean-miss outcome (unseal, emit nothing, stop), whatever its state. Replace "the guard's row is `running` and names `row.PodAssignment`" with "the guard's row names `row.PodAssignment`" — BECAUSE a landed Idle write clears the pod and the pod stays `bound` until step 4, so neither `claimIdle` (Idle phase only) nor `rebindReserved` nor a held-pod resume (needs `holdsPod`) can put it back — ALTERNATIVES: adding an interrupt-`suspended` clause to the round-8 enumeration (rejected: the same state-keyed answer one row wider; the next unlisted state that keeps the pod would be the next finding); unseal on every post-error miss (rejected again: a landed-then-restored row on a fresh pod is `running` too).
CORRECTS [non-spec.8.fix-design-G2.1 FACT "in the remaining other miss rows (an interrupt suspension ...) the session is no longer `running` on the sealed pod"]: an interrupt write (`transitionInterrupt`, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_transitions.go:119; interrupt.go:84-89) keeps `PodAssignment`, and a held-pod resume then writes `running` on the same sealed pod. Under the pod-keyed rule that row unseals; only rows that no longer name the pod keep the seal.
WATCHOUT: the discriminating keep-seal case is "applied, then error, then the row is `running` naming ANOTHER pod". It fails both wrong implementations (unseal on any post-error `running` row; unseal on any post-error miss that is not the landed form). A `resume_pending` variant fails only the second, so it is not needed. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Tests that land with CODE-6, tier-1 session-server bullet.
WATCHOUT: the in-flight gate test discriminates on the second `SealSession` call (a repeat seal succeeds, so without the gate the second run proceeds and later misses and unseals after the first run's write). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 `SuspendIdle` opening and **Session seal.** Handlers ("a repeat on a sealed entry succeeds").

DECISION: no findings under the reliability lens in non-spec round 9 — BECAUSE the r8-to-now delta (SuspendIdle step 3 unseals when a post-store-error repeat misses on a row still `running` on `row.PodAssignment`; the errResumeHeldPodReleased answer moved from CODE-4 item 8 to CODE-6 item 6; per-session seal tests; tier-5 co-tenant assertion) is sound: a landed idle write leaves the row podless and the pod unreleased, so no restore can return the row to `running` on that pod before step 4, and "running on the same pod" after a store error therefore means the write did not land — ALTERNATIVES: filing the unpaced step-3 repeat (Traps **Commit repeat pacing.**, do not refile); filing a seal left on an interrupt-suspended-then-resumed session (close variant of the refuted interrupt-racing-the-seal item and of owner decision OD-R9-B).

### [non-spec.9.review-test-coverage.1]
DECISION: filed two test-coverage findings on CODE-6 item 4 **`SuspendIdle`.** (1) No test pins the per-replica in-flight gate for `SuspendIdle`. Watchdog `Tick` runs every 5 s (pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:166) and a checkpoint may take up to 60 s, so the gate is what stops a second run whose guard misses after the first run's write from calling `unsealSession` before the first run's step 4 release. (2) No test pins the keep-seal branch of step 3 for a post-store-error miss on a `running` row naming a different pod, or on a non-`running` row. Today's step-3 tests also pass under "unseal on any `running` row", which is the alternative the review log rejects at line 1326. — BECAUSE the lens requires a fail-closed path to have a test that discriminates it — ALTERNATIVES: refiling the 60 s retry-skip test (rejected; it was already rejected in the review log at line 1469 as non-spec pacing); a status-change-emission assertion for the success case (rejected as a nice-to-have).
FACT: `SuspendIdle`'s step 3 has four post-write branches. Three of them have tests in the tier-1 bullet at non-spec-changes.md:610: a clean miss, an applied-then-error write, and the new not-applied-then-`running`-same-pod case (round 9). A context end also has a test. The "any other miss after a store error" branch has none. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:319, :610
WATCHOUT: TestReleaseSuspendedPodRunsOffTheCaller pins the in-flight gate for `ReleaseSuspendedPod` only. Do not read it as also covering `SuspendIdle`, which has its own entry gate even though both share the same records. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:316, :610

### [non-spec.11.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 4 `SuspendIdle` step 3 guard gains `CoordinationGeneration == bind.CoordinationGeneration` — BECAUSE the row write is the CAS §10.1.5 names as the stale-replica signal, and without the conjunct a replica that lost REG-COORDLEASE after its D1 check could land `suspended` and release the pod while the new holder's own run misses, unseals under its fenced generation, and lets a delivery reach the runtime before the stale release ends that turn — ALTERNATIVES: listing-row generation (correct only by D1-after-listing ordering; the binding stamp is what the seal and pod fence use); a generation check in releaseReportedBinding/PodExecutor.Release (widens a shared path); taking the delivery lock (reintroduces the nesting redesign 9 removed); re-checking D1 before step 3 (not atomic with the write).
FACT: `podsession.BindResult.CoordinationGeneration` and `sessionstore.Session.CoordinationGeneration` are both `int64` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindrequest.go (BindResult), pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go (Session).
WATCHOUT: the step-3 "counts as landed" sentence (a miss after a store error whose guard row is `suspended`/`Idle`/podless) does not check the generation, so a landed write by a now-superseded holder followed by a takeover is still classified as landed. Left unchanged by design this round; a later round should judge whether it is a finding — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 step 3.
WATCHOUT: a generation miss with no prior store error takes the existing clean-miss outcome (unseal, which the adapter's fence refuses and the caller logs). Do not add a generation-specific miss branch; the discriminating test assertion is "no executor release and no session.pod_released_during_suspension".

### [non-spec.11.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SuspendIdle step 3 guard gains `&& CoordinationGeneration == bind.CoordinationGeneration` (the stamp on the binding D2 read), not `row.CoordinationGeneration` as the reviewer suggested — BECAUSE the seal and the pod fence already run under the binding stamp (sealSession sends bind.CoordinationGeneration), the binding-stamp-vs-row comparison is the existing §10.1.5 stale-replica test the stream-failure report makes (podsession/bindrequest.go `CoordinationGeneration` doc comment), and the stamp is independent of when sweepIdle listed the row. A `running` row's generation moves only on a takeover CAS (resume entry and terminal collapse start from other states), so the holder never misses spuriously. ALTERNATIVES: listing-row generation (works only because D1 follows the listing; ties safety to call order); a generation check in releaseReportedBinding/PodExecutor.Release (widens a shared release path for one caller; the write is the CAS §10.1.5 names); taking the delivery lock in SuspendIdle (reintroduces the lock nesting redesign 9 removed).
DECISION: this differs in kind from the round-8/9 rewrites — those reclassified the post-store-error miss outcomes; this adds the missing fence conjunct to the guard predicate, and leaves the outcome sentences untouched.
WATCHOUT: the step-3 "counts as landed" form (row `suspended`, `Idle`, empty pod after a store error) does not check the generation, so after a store error a stale replica can read a NEW holder's landed write as its own and also run step 4. Both replicas then release a sealed pod already written podless, so no turn ends; not filed as part of this fix. A later round that finds it should add the generation to that landed form rather than to a new branch. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 SuspendIdle step 3, "counts as landed".
FACT: BindResult.CoordinationGeneration is stamped by stampBindingGeneration before every registry publish; Binder results leave it zero. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_registration.go:82-90, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindrequest.go:169-178
FACT: PodExecutor.Release → binder.Release makes no generation-checked adapter call, so only the row write can fence a stale idle release. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:257-271

### [non-spec.11.review-fresh.1]
FACT: The round-11 CODE-6 item 4 step 3 unseal predicate is "the guard's row names `row.PodAssignment`", whatever its state. It covers the same-pod `running` row and the interrupt-`suspended` row. A landed idle write is podless, and a restore never reclaims the released pod, so on a session pool the predicate cannot misread a landed write. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:319 and :305 ("a restore claims an idle pod ... never the pod step 4 releases").
WATCHOUT: On a concurrent pool, `PodAssignment` names the shared pod, as CODE-4 item 5 step 3 already notes. A landed idle write followed by a restore onto another slot of that same pod would therefore read as "not landed" and get an extra `UnsealSession`. The outcome is the same as the keep-seal branch (no release, orphan GC), and the stale-generation fence refuses that unseal on the new entry, so it is not a finding. Do not re-file it. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:153, :326 (coordinator_handoff_stale check).
FACT: Every adapter write of a `message` frame to the runtime goes through `writeSessionEnvelope` (`session.go:234`, `attach.go:54/198/259`). The other `WriteEnvelope` sites write only `session_start`, `session_end`, or a heartbeat, so CODE-6 item 18's single increment site holds. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/sessionframes.go:115,135; pkg/adapter/heartbeat.go:153.
FACT: `envelopeWriteStatus` today formats "%s: %v" with the caller's `what` first. CODE-6 item 4 **Refusal.** therefore requires a special case so that the message begins with `session_sealed:`, which the executor prefix match depends on. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:292-297.

### [non-spec.11.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The round-10 rewrite of CODE-6 item 4 `SuspendIdle` step 3 keys the unseal on "the guard's row names `row.PodAssignment`". Every post-store-error miss classifies consistently with the new tier-1 cases: a landed write clears the pod, an interrupt cannot write a podless `suspended` row back onto the pod, and a restore cannot land on the unreleased pod P, because P's adapter entry still exists (pkg/adapter/slotsession.go refuses a second claim on a started entry). So "names P" implies "did not land". EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 **`SuspendIdle`.** step 3; Testing, CODE-6 tier-1 session-server bullet.
WATCHOUT: One residual case remains. The write does not land, a store error follows, and the row is then restored onto another pod Q. The code keeps the seal on P, while SPEC-5f reads "lifts the seal". The seal sits on P's dead or orphaned entry, and Q's new entry starts unsealed, so nothing can observe it. Do not file this as a finding. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**; SPEC-5ab **Session seal.** "a new registry entry for the session starts unsealed".
UNVERIFIED: summary OD-R9-B **Ground.** says "a turn that an interrupt left outstanding" makes the re-seal refuse. A sealed session writes no `message`, so its count stays 0 across an interrupt and a held-pod resume, and the case looks unreachable. It sits in an owner decision entry and is not a citation, so it was not filed. A decisions-phase agent should check it.

### [non-spec.11.review-performance.1]

The 300 s leader backlog re-check (one HeldLen per podless row) is small next to them. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:166 (5 s tick), :1075 and :1142 (List with an empty filter); pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:303 (Sweep lists all rows) and :318 (leases.Get per non-terminal row); pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:576 (List has no LIMIT).
WATCHOUT: Do not file "the backlog re-check / Sweeper per-row Redis GET does not scale to Tier 4" as new to 0092. That full-scan pattern predates the proposal. A finding here has to show load that the proposal creates beyond the existing per-tick full scans. EVIDENCE: the same lines.
DECISION: Returned no findings in round 11 under the performance lens — BECAUSE the step-3 repeat copies the settled CODE-4 item 5.2 commit repeat, a refused seal costs one adapter gRPC per over-idle session per 5 s tick with no store write, and the in-flight gate bounds SuspendIdle to one run per session — ALTERNATIVES: an unbounded-retry-during-Postgres-failover finding was rejected because only suspensions whose write was in flight at the outage loop, and the pattern is already settled.

### [non-spec.11.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding this round — BECAUSE the r10→r11 diff (CODE-6 item 4 step 3 unseal branch widened to any guard row naming `row.PodAssignment`, plus the matching tier-1 cases) only lifts a seal on a session whose pod was not released, and a landed write clears `PodAssignment`, so a row naming P after a landed write is unreachable (podless Idle rows never take the held-pod branch; restores claim a fresh pod) — ALTERNATIVES: filing an unseal on a terminal or recovering row that still names P (rejected: harmless, adapter entry gone or pod not reused).
FACT: delegation budget keys fail closed on TTL expiry: `budget_reserve.lua`/`budget_return.lua` check the `{root}:dlg:tokens` sentinel and return `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED`, which cascade-cancels the tree and fails the root, so a failed `RearmTree` (CODE-6 item 14, WARN only) cannot silently reset a budget ceiling — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:408-417.
FACT: the adapter gRPC server has no per-method authorization list beyond `coordinatorHoldAllowedMethods` (pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:52-55) and `credredact.go`'s redaction set, so the new Seal/Unseal RPCs need no auth-list entry — EVIDENCE: grep FullMethod in pkg/adapter.
FACT: the tenant-deletion Phase 4 half of SPEC-5aa is filed as RECORDS-1 item 7(p), not built; do not file it as a missing edit site — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:504.
WATCHOUT: the turn count is decremented from runtime-written `response` frames, so a runtime can hold a session out of idle suspension indefinitely (bounded by `maxSessionAge`) or, on a concurrent pod, decrement a co-hosted session's count; neither crosses a trust boundary the runtime does not already hold over its own sessions, so it is not a security-bound finding under this lens.

### [mechanisms.reconcile.1]
DECISION: the idle-suspension attempt belongs to the coordinator incarnation named by `bind.CoordinationGeneration`, and `classifyIdleWrite` on the locked row decides every try of the suspend write; a generation miss sends the pod nothing — BECAUSE every takeover, every entry into `resuming`, and every mid-resume snapshot-close write advances the row's generation, the pod release is not generation-checked, and the post-error row-form list had no single rule — ALTERNATIVES: a `SuspendedAt` identity (not needed, since only this incarnation writes `Idle` at its generation); no generation condition (an adopter's turn could have its pod released).
DECISION: OD-R9-A resolved (a) and OD-R9-B resolved (generation condition kept, fence unchanged); SPEC-5ab is the one spec home of the unlifted-seal bound; SPEC-5e counts a client message only once the adapter writes it.
FACT: pgstore `Update` runs the mutator under `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` and floors `coordination_generation`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go, `Update`
FACT: `CoordinatorFence` changes no per-session adapter state. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/coordination.go, `CoordinatorFence`
FACT: a sealed entry's turn count stays at 0, because the seal requires 0 and the only increment is in the seal-check hold. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5ab **Session seal.**; non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 **Refusal.**
CORRECTS [spec.10.review-reliability.1 WATCHOUT "variants of the stuck seal are all OD-R9-B territory"]: a stale coordinator's idle write after adoption is a defect the generation condition excludes; an interrupt landing on a sealed session cannot leave a turn outstanding; the fence variant is settled by OD-R9-B.
CORRECTS [Standing Settled **Idle atomicity.**] and [Standing Settled **Adapter seal.**]: as rewritten above.
CORRECTS [Standing Settled **Owner answers.**]: OD-A `StampSync` is withdrawn by Decision 25; the summary OD-R9-A entry it cited is deleted.
CORRECTS [Standing Settled **Session-row read authority.**]: the idle declining mutator's sentinel is `errIdleWriteNotApplied`, returned by `classifyIdleWrite`; `errIdleSuspendSuperseded` is deleted.
CORRECTS [the CORRECTS entry quoting "No store error lifts the seal"]: a post-error miss lifts the seal only at the attempt's own generation with the row naming the pod.
CORRECTS [summary OD-R9-B "a turn that an interrupt left outstanding" exception]: unreachable on a sealed entry; the interrupted turn is the seal-refused case SPEC-5f states.
CORRECTS [f3.human-decisions.OD-R9-B OPEN] and [reconcile.deliverables.3 OPEN entries on credrouter, `dlq.go`, and the `Idle` fixtures]: closed by Decisions 25 and 26 and by CODE-4 item 20.6, CODE-6 item 12, and the retargeted tests.
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce branches keyed on whether an earlier `Update` erred, or a row-form list; extend `classifyIdleWrite` and its table test instead.

### [spec.13.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: The adapter carries the turn count through a checkpoint: each checkpoint records the peak count during capture with the session file, and a `Resume`-created entry starts at the recorded count (0 when no session file is restored). The one normative home is SPEC-5ab item 2 **Session seal.**, and CODE-6 item 18.3 **Checkpoint record.** carries constraints only. BECAUSE a restored runtime continues an in-flight turn with no new `message` (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md **Duplicate spawn across parent recovery.**, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:426-428), so a zero start lets an idle firing seal and release mid-turn. ALTERNATIVES: a ready_for_input gate (no carrier for the signal, so it disables idle suspension on every resumed session); starting at 1 (sticks forever after a between-turns restore); settling on the first `response` (responses carry no correlation); proto fields plus a checkpoint column (new wire surface for adapter-internal state); a runtime `continuing` flag (changes the runtime contract); accepting the window (caller directive forbids it).
WATCHOUT: The record must precede the session file in archive stream order, so a tail-truncated partial reassembly that restores the file also restores the record, and it must sit outside `/sessions/{sessionId}` or any other runtime-writable directory, because a runtime that forges it to zero reopens a mid-turn release. Do not add a decay or timeout to a stuck restored count: `maxSessionAge` bounds it, and that is the fail-safe direction. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 18.3.
FACT: The idle suspension's own checkpoint is taken under the seal at count 0, so the normal suspend-then-resume path records 0 and seals again normally after resume. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5ab item 2 (`SealSession` seals only at count 0).

### [spec.13.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the Resume-created zero-count gap by carrying the count through the checkpoint: each checkpoint records, with the session file, the highest count the session held while it was captured, and a `Resume`-created entry starts at the count recorded with the session file it restores (zero when it restores none). One clause plus one sentence in SPEC-5ab item 2, plus a CODE-6 item 18 sub-item and one tier-1 test. — BECAUSE the spec does establish autonomous continuation of an in-flight turn after a restore (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md **Duplicate spawn across parent recovery.**: "re-issues any `delegate_task` call whose tool result is absent"; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md **External side effects across recovery.**), so the finding is valid; the adapter alone knows the count at checkpoint time and alone restores the archive, so no proto, column, or gateway change is needed. — ALTERNATIVES: (1) the reviewer's gate on `ready_for_input` since the `Resume` (rejected: the carrier of that signal is `unstated`, spec/29_communication-scenarios.md step 8 near :482, and the code defers it, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_held_pod.go:58-59; built literally, every entry a `Resume` created refuses every seal, so a session resumed once from a podless suspension never idle-suspends again); (2) start every `Resume`-created entry at one (rejected: a restore from a between-turns checkpoint, the common case, never sees the extra `response`, so the count sticks at one forever on that entry); (3) settle a resumed entry on its first `response` (rejected: `response` frames carry no message correlation, spec/28_communication-channels.md **Outbound: `response`**, so a hidden turn's `response` and a new message's `response` cannot be told apart; a message arriving mid-continuation gets sealed mid-turn); (4) a `turn_outstanding` field on `CheckpointSummary` and `ResumeRequest` with a checkpoint-row column (rejected: two wire fields and a column for state the adapter can carry in its own archive); (5) a runtime-declared `continuing` flag on `session_started` (rejected: runtime contract change for every runtime and conformance battery).
FACT: the idle suspension's own checkpoint is taken under the seal at count 0 with no `message` writable, so the main idle-suspend → resume path records 0 and is unaffected; the fix only changes restores from periodic, eviction, drain, rotation, and interrupt-release checkpoints. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** ("Once the session is sealed, the gateway checkpoints")
FACT: `response` frames carry only `type`, `output`, `sessionId`; no message correlation exists on CH-MSGSOCK. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md **Outbound: `response`** (~:753-767)
FACT: the session file is captured from the runtime-writable `/sessions/{sessionId}` tmpfs under `workspace.SessionsPrefix` (pkg/adapter/slot.go:493-525, pkg/adapter/server.go:85-93); a count record placed there is forgeable by the runtime toward 0, which would reopen a mid-turn release.
WATCHOUT: a runtime that does not continue a restored in-flight turn leaves a recorded count of 1 stuck on that entry, so every idle firing is refused and `maxSessionAge` bounds the session (SPEC-5f refusal outcome). This is the fail-safe direction; do not "fix" it by decaying the count or by a timeout, which reopens the mid-turn release. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f
WATCHOUT: record the count before the session file in the archive's stream order, or a tail-truncated partial reassembly can restore a session file without its record and start the entry at zero mid-turn. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md CH-OBJSTORE row (partial reassembly)
CORRECTS [review-log line ~1106 WATCHOUT on the Session seal count]: the spec does establish autonomous continuation after a restore; §8.10 **Duplicate spawn across parent recovery.** says the restored parent re-issues any `delegate_task` whose result is absent from the checkpoint. The filing it declined is valid.

### [spec.13.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in the round-13 spec staging — BECAUSE the redesign-10 delta (SPEC-5f generation conjunct on the suspend write and no unseal on a generation miss, SPEC-5ab as the home of the unlifted-seal bound, SPEC-5e counting a client message once the adapter writes it) adds no store write, watch, lock, or RPC. The conjunct rides the existing one-row `Update`; a generation miss leaves the seal, and the next 5 s watchdog firing on the current coordinator re-seals (count stays 0) and completes, so a takeover or a Redis-lease-loss re-adoption costs at most one extra tick. During a Postgres outage no takeover can increment the generation (spec/10_gateway-internals.md §10.1.1 Postgres-unavailable item 1), so the conjunct cannot strand an in-flight suspension then — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing messages refused by a left seal waiting in the DLQ until the next idle firing (rejected: latency trade-off already on the refuted list, bounded by `maxSessionAge`); (2) filing Redis loss of those held messages (rejected: Standing **Refuted: store-outage degradation.**); (3) filing the per-qualifying-event `last_agent_activity_at` stamp as new write load (rejected: SPEC-5e only moves the stamp point from receipt to adapter write; the per-event Postgres stamp predates 0092).
FACT: §10.1.2 step 1 CAS precedes step 2 `CoordinatorFence`, so SPEC-5f's "advances `coordination_generation` before the adopting coordinator sends the pod any RPC" holds. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:37-38

### [spec.13.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in spec round 13 — BECAUSE crash, store-error, and takeover paths through the redesign-10 idle suspension (SPEC-5f, SPEC-5ab) all end in a bounded state. (1) A crash between seal and write leaves the seal. The peer adopts the `running` row (SPEC-2a). Refused messages do not reset `last_agent_activity_at` (SPEC-5e), so the adopter's next idle firing re-seals at count 0 and completes. (2) A crash after the write leaves the pod unreferenced, and orphan-claim GC reclaims it. The leader re-check covers the backlog, and in-memory inbox loss is already accepted. (3) A stale replica's late `UnsealSession` after a takeover cannot lift the adopter's seal, because the pod rejects any gateway-to-pod RPC with a stale generation. — ALTERNATIVES: filing a stale replica's checkpoint-failure unseal racing the adopter's suspension (rejected: generation fencing on every pod RPC); filing the interrupt hold release (SPEC-5b), which has no generation conjunct (rejected: the row is `suspended`, so no delivery is made, and SPEC-1f item 3 covers a release by a replica that lost the lease); filing messages held behind a seal that was not lifted (rejected: latency bounded by the next idle firing or `maxSessionAge`, already refuted).
FACT: the pod validates `coordination_generation` on every gateway-to-pod RPC and rejects a stale one, so `SealSession`/`UnsealSession` from a replica that lost the session are refused once the adopter's `CoordinatorFence` lands — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:237-238
FACT: concurrent idle attempts on one replica at one generation are excluded by the per-replica in-flight record that `SuspendIdle` shares with `ReleaseSuspendedPod` — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 **`SuspendIdle`.** ("when a release or an idle suspension of the session is in flight on this replica")
USEFUL [Standing **Refuted: store-outage degradation.**]: it saved refiling the loss of `suspended` DLQ entries held in Redis for 7 days.

### [spec.13.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding on the staged spec edits — BECAUSE every changed control checked holds: the suspension release drains a non-recycling pod and recycles a tenant-pinned one (stricter than the current "back to the pool", spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:229); §12.8 erasure gains the DLQ and durable-inbox keys before SessionStore through the existing `RedisConcernSessionData` concern (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:411); BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED stays sentinel-detected and fail-closed (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:408); generation-scoped lease release only ever revokes extra (never leaks a lease that a restore recorded) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the adapter turn count as a pod self-report bound (rejected: it guards the session's own pod release, not a cross-session or residual-state bound, and maxSessionAge still caps a runtime that withholds `response`).
FACT: erasure verification in §12.8 checks only the erasure salt, so the SPEC-5aa scope row needs no verification-step edit — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:895
WATCHOUT: SPEC-1g's "never returns a lease that a later restore assigned" holds only for restore-recorded and replacement leases; a zero-recorded lease assigned mid-session after a restore can be revoked by a stale release. That direction over-revokes (availability), not a leak; do not file it as a security regression.

### [spec.13.review-single-source.1]
FACT: SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** states the SPEC-5ab seal contract twice in its own paragraph: "The adapter seals the session only when no turn is outstanding, and while the session is sealed it writes no message to the runtime, whichever replica delivers it" (right after the §4.7.1 **Session seal.** citation), and again in the safety argument, "The adapter places the seal only when no turn is outstanding". SPEC-5ab item 2 is the home ("The adapter refuses it while a turn is outstanding ... While a session is sealed, the adapter writes no `message` frame"). Filed under (g); the fix deletes both SPEC-5f copies and keeps the citations. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f and SPEC-5ab item 2
DECISION: did not file "Another suspension attempt lifts the seal only after a takeover" (SPEC-5f) against SPEC-5ab's "the next idle firing on its coordinating replica seals it again" — BECAUSE it holds if "attempt" is read as scoped to one coordinator generation, which is how the caller frames it; a same-replica repeat after a failed UnsealSession is serialized by the in-flight record and does not break safety — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a (c) contradiction (ambiguous, likely refuted).
DECISION: did not file the DLQ scoring rule at three sites (dead-letter table, migration step 2, §12.4 key table), the §28.5.1 Timing restatement of the 5 s deadline, or the Design-section route list — BECAUSE they follow existing spec conventions (key registry, channel cards) or are a summary description of a deliverable.
WATCHOUT: the Edge-cases bullet "No outcome releases a pod with a turn outstanding, including during a Postgres failover" repeats the SPEC-5f sentence "The release never ends a turn, including during a Postgres failover"; it is one clause and was not filed. If SPEC-5f's invariant wording changes, change that bullet too or reduce it to a pointer. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Edge cases, "An adapter that refuses the seal" bullet

### [spec.14.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: Each checkpoint archive carries one turn record (`adapter/turns`, last entry); the leading record (`adapter/turns-lead`) is deleted. A restore that read chunks but no record sets per-entry `turnsUnknown`, and `SealSession` refuses while it is set — BECAUSE a record written before the session file finishes archiving cannot bound the `message` frames that file holds, so any count taken from it admits a mid-turn seal and contradicts SPEC-5f — ALTERNATIVES: holding `message` writes from the leading record until the session file is archived (second mechanism, blocking wait with wake-up on every capture abort path); flooring the count at 1 (unsound: several post-lead messages drain it to 0 mid-turn); accepting the omission (the excluded mid-turn window).
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce a leading turn record or a count floor for a cut restore; do not encode the refusal as a sentinel count (a later `response` decrements it). A `Resume` with no chunks starts at 0 and is not a cut restore. maxSessionAge bounds the refusal. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes SPEC-5ab **Session seal.**; non-spec CODE-6 item 18.3.
CORRECTS [standing context, Session seal trap paragraph]: "Keep both turn records and their order" is withdrawn; there is one turn record, and a restore cut before it refuses the seal.
FACT: Today the refusal is unreachable: `ExtractTree` errors on a truncated stream (pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go:170) and `Resume` aborts (pkg/adapter/resume.go:243); RECORDS-1 7(s) files it. — EVIDENCE: those lines.
DEFERRED [proposals/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway.implementation-checklist.md]: any step naming `TestTurnRecordsBracketSessionFile_spec_4_7_1` or a leading turn record is stale; the test is now `TestTurnRecordIsLastArchiveEntry_spec_4_7_1` and there is one record (grep found no hit at edit time, so likely a no-op).

### [spec.14.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Delete the leading turn record. The archive carries one turn record, as its last entry; `Resume` sets the count from it, to zero when no archive is restored, and when the restored archive ends before the record (a §10.1.7 **Reassembly on resume.** end-truncated restore) the adapter refuses every `SealSession` for that entry, so `maxSessionAge` bounds it — BECAUSE no record written before the session file ends can bound the `message` frames that file holds, so any count read from a pre-session-file record admits a mid-turn seal; refusing the seal is the existing fail-safe outcome of "a turn the runtime never answers" — ALTERNATIVES: (a) the reviewer's write hold from the leading record until the session file is archived: a new blocking wait on the message path with wake-up obligations on every capture abort path, stalling delivery for the length of the session-file archive on every checkpoint, to serve a restore path the tree cannot even run today (RECORDS-1 7(s)); (b) the count floored at 1 on a cut restore: unsound, because several `message` frames can be written after the leading record and the runtime answers them in order, so the count reaches 0 mid-turn.
FACT: `ExtractTree` returns an error on any read failure and `Resume` aborts on it, so no partial-workspace restore runs in the tree; the refusal costs nothing until RECORDS-1 7(s) is fixed. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go:170, pkg/adapter/resume.go:243.
CORRECTS [spec.13 WATCHOUT "The record must precede the session file in archive stream order"]: a record that precedes the session file does not bound the frames the file holds; the record is the archive's last entry, and a restore cut before it refuses the seal. The half of that WATCHOUT about the record sitting outside any runtime-writable directory, and about adding no decay to a stuck count, stays true.
WATCHOUT: Distinguish "no archive" (count 0) from "an archive cut before its record" (seal refused) in `Resume`: both see found=false from `ExtractTree`, and only the second restored chunks. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 18.3.

### [spec.14.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: a checkpoint taken while `turnsUnknown` holds writes no turn record (SPEC-5ab item 2; CODE-6 item 18.3 record callback returns none) — BECAUSE a record taken from the post-cut count (often 0) lets a later §7.3 resume from that periodic checkpoint set `turns = 0` and seal mid-turn — ALTERNATIVES: persisting a separate "unknown" marker in the archive (new wire format, more text; absence of the record already means unknown on restore)
FACT: the SPEC-5ab refusal predicate now reads "has no turn record" rather than "ends before its turn record", so a clean archive with no `adapter/turns` entry triggers it; item 5's `Checkpoint` row now says the archive ends with the record "except as **Session seal.** below states" — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5ab items 2 and 5
FACT: today's `Resume` aborts on an `ExtractTree` read error and releases the slot, so the end-truncated path never reaches the refusal; only a complete archive without a record does — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/resume.go:126-131, pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go:169-170
WATCHOUT: test cases for the refusal must build a complete archive without a record (`ArchiveTree` with no callback), never a truncated stream; RECORDS-1 7(s) and item 8's "archive cut before its turn record" exclusion name only the truncated case and stay true — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md RECORDS-1 items 7(s), 8; Testing `TestResumeStartsAtRecordedTurnCount_spec_4_7_1`
Citer sweep: "turn record", "ends before", "cut before" across all proposal files; fixed summary decision 16 ("Each checkpoint archive carries" and "A restore cut before the record"); docs item "recorded at the end of the checkpoint" left as true.

### [spec.14.review-fresh.1]
DECISION: filed one finding. SPEC-5f says "The release never ends a turn", but the round-13 turn-record design in SPEC-5ab sets a restored count from the leading record when the archive is cut inside the session file. The Edge-cases bullet "A partial-workspace restore cut inside the session file" accepts that this count omits a `message` written while the session file was archived. Such a runtime continues the turn at count 0, so a later idle seal is granted mid-turn. That is an accepted mid-turn release window, which the caller directive forbids. — BECAUSE the leading record is written before the session file, and the runtime can record a message delivered during that capture in the file that is restored — ALTERNATIVES: filing the peak-count over-count that sticks a session at 1 when a turn ends during capture (rejected: fail-safe, bounded by maxSessionAge, which the proposal accepts); filing the INTERRUPT_TIMEOUT hold release ending a turn (rejected: pre-existing interrupt-suspension behavior, and SPEC-5f's invariant is scoped to the idle release).
FACT: every RPC other than `CoordinatorFence` is refused in adapter hold state as a universal, so `SealSession`/`UnsealSession` need no hold-state list edit. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:252-253, spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:1546
FACT: `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/workspace` streams the checkpoint's chunk sequence, which already carries the `sessions/` root, so the new `adapter/` records ride the same pre-existing exposure. That is not a 0092 spec edit site. EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:700, pkg/adapter/workspace/tree.go:37-43
WATCHOUT: a fix for the session-file cut must not add a decay or a timeout to the count. Prefer to defer `message` writes while the session file is being archived, or to restore a fail-safe nonzero count. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5ab item 2; non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 18.3
USEFUL [spec.13.fix-G1.1]: its record-ordering WATCHOUT located the exact window this finding names.

### [spec.14.mechanism-respec.turn-count.1]
DECISION: the turn count crosses a checkpoint as metadata. CheckpointSummary.turn_count goes to checkpoint_manifest.turn_count (partial=false only, migration 0184; derive copies the parent's) and then to ResumeRequest.turn_count. Resume sets the count; no count with chunks means unknown, which refuses the seal until the next `response` — BECAUSE the archive record needed a stream-order rule, a peak, a capture flag, and a goroutine join, and it still could not cover a truncated archive. A metadata field written once at completion has no ordering and is absent on every partial row by construction — ALTERNATIVES: the trailing archive record of [spec.14.fix-design-G1.1] (rejected: those four mechanisms and the `adapter/` entry exposed through GET /workspace); a count read before the capture (rejected: undercounts `message` frames that the restored file holds); a peak over the capture carried as metadata (open decision OD-1 of this redesign; default not taken).
CORRECTS [standing context Traps, Session seal paragraph]: "Keep both turn records and their order" and the archive-goroutine join are withdrawn. No turn record exists.
CORRECTS [spec.14.fix-G1.1, spec.14.fix-design-G1.1, spec.14.follow-up-fix.1]: their record placement, `turnsUnknown`-writes-no-record, and `TestTurnRecordIsLastArchiveEntry_spec_4_7_1` are superseded. `turnsUnknown` survives as the unknown-count bit, cleared by the first `response`.
FACT: the tree has no turn count today. The finalise and resume sites the carriage touches are uploadDriver.onSummary and finaliseAbort (pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/uploaddriver.go), deriveChunkedCheckpoint (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go), Store.Finalise and MemoryStore.Finalise (partialmanifeststore.go), pgstore.Store.Finalise, sessionserver.CheckpointManifestWriter (checkpoint_seams.go), resumechunks.Resolve, resolveResumeChunks and resolveFullCheckpointFallback (resume_checkpoint.go), checkpointResumeRequest (resume_rebind.go), Binder.Resume (podsession/resume.go), adapterclient.Client.Resume, and the adapter's Resume (pkg/adapter/resume.go, restoreChunks before Runtime.Start). Finalise also has seven test callers outside the store packages; CODE-6 item 18.3 names them.
WATCHOUT: do not move the count back into the archive, and do not read it on any path other than the end of the archive stream. A Finalise caller other than onSummary and derive passes nil.

### [spec.16.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in spec round 16 — BECAUSE the redesign-12 turn-count carriage adds no store write, watch, or RPC: the count rides the existing finalisation `UPDATE` of the `checkpoint_manifest` row (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:148, step 3 "Finalisation") and the existing resume-selection read (:171), and the adapter count lives in adapter memory, so a gateway coordinator handoff or a Redis reset leaves it intact. A Postgres failure during finalisation leaves the row `partial = true`, whose restore yields an unknown count (seal refused), which is fail-safe — ALTERNATIVES: (1) per-tick `SealSession` re-asks on a session whose count stays above zero (rejected: a cheap gateway-to-pod RPC per 5 s tick per stuck session, no stated budget broken); (2) repeated idle checkpoint attempts during a MinIO outage (rejected: CODE-6 item 4 `SuspendIdle` gates on "last checkpoint failure is under 60 s old", so the rate is idle sessions / 60 s and each attempt is two Postgres writes, well inside the ~1,300/s Tier 3 Postgres figure at spec/12_storage-architecture.md:72; the gate is non-spec anyway); (3) `RearmTree` EXPIRE fan-out (rejected: maxTreeSize default 50, spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:125, already judged).
FACT: the `lenny-gateway-leader` Lease name the SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** bullet cites under §12.5 is in the spec, inside GC concurrency rule 1. A grep with a truncating `head` can hide it. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:358
USEFUL [spec.13.review-performance.1]: its 5 s watchdog re-seal math and the observation that no takeover can increment the generation during a Postgres outage carried over unchanged to redesign 12.
USEFUL [spec.15.review-fresh.1]: its FACT that every checkpoint trigger opens and finalises a manifest row saved re-deriving where the count is written.

### [spec.16.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings on the staged spec edits in round 16. BECAUSE every crash, restart, and failover path through the redesign-12 turn-count carriage ends in a known count or in an unknown count, and an unknown count only refuses the seal. Paths traced: a gateway crash between the adapter's terminal summary and `Finalise` (the row is never finalised `partial = false`, so the restore passes no count and the count is unknown); a finalise that fails during a Postgres failover (the selector restores an older complete row, whose count and archive are a pair); legacy rows written before migration 0184 (NULL, so unknown); a held-pod resume and a `CoordinatorFence` takeover (the registry entry and its count survive, and no `Resume` runs); a snapshotless rebuild and a no-archive restore (count 0, with no carried conversation). The idle seal also ends bounded when `SealSession` times out after the seal landed, or when `UnsealSession` fails after a `last_agent_activity_at` miss: the seal stays until the next idle firing re-seals and completes, and refused messages do not reset the idle clock (SPEC-5e). ALTERNATIVES: filing the 7-day Redis-only DLQ hold for `suspended` against the Postgres-backed `session_dlq_archive` that `awaiting_client_action` gets (rejected: Standing **Refuted: store-outage degradation.** and **Not filed** `session_dlq_archive` replay); filing an unspecified backoff for the driver's pre-claim retries (rejected: the spec states that it retries later and that §7.3 step 3c bounds the wait, and the non-spec Testing pins a jittered backoff); filing derive's start path for a mid-turn parent (rejected: speculative, and the derive count copy is non-spec and already an OPEN).
FACT: §7.2 delivery path 1 resolves a `lenny/request_input` answer directly and writes no stdin `message`, so `input_required` answers never move the adapter's turn count. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:326
FACT: §4.4.5's minimal state record resume (`conversation_only`) restores no archive, so under SPEC-5ab its count starts at 0. The record holds only the last completed prompt/response pair, and the driver has no minimal-state branch (RECORDS-1 7(q)). EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:284-292
USEFUL [spec.13.review-reliability.1]: its seal-crash trace (seal left by a crashed attempt, re-seal on the adopter's idle firing, and a stale unseal refused by generation fencing) still holds after redesign 12. Re-verifying it took one read.
USEFUL [spec.15.review-fresh.1]: its WATCHOUT on the §10.1.7 in-pod fallback, which yields an unknown count rather than an unsafe one, saved a refile.

### [spec.16.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security findings on the staged spec edits in round 16 — BECAUSE every staged control change either keeps the gateway as the authority (maxSessionAge accrual, suspended lifetime, backlog predicate, lease generation scope) or affects only the session's own liveness (the adapter turn count and seal) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the turn count as a pod self-reported bound (rejected: a false count only suspends or pins the reporting session's own pod, and maxSessionAge, which the gateway measures, bounds it; no isolation or quota bound reads it).

The §6.2 pause table predates 0092, and SPEC-5g's 7-day lifetime tightens the previously unbounded podless suspension, so this is not a 0092 regression — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/playground_caps.go:129-132, spec/27_web-playground.md:203
FACT: §6.1 requires the credential file to be written before the adapter writes session_start, which matches SPEC-1g's AssignCredentials-before-Resume order — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:26, spec/04_system-components.md:864
FACT: DeleteByUser terminates no sessions (step 1 only releases coordination leases), so any DLQ enqueue after step 10 can recreate a key. That race class predates 0092 (no key-level EXPIRE on the DLQ) and is not filed — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:849,862

ALTERNATIVES: filing a reduction, rejected because neither one subsumes the other.

### [non-spec.13.review-performance.1]

FACT: Every session-row writer goes through `Store.Update`, so CODE-1's accrual funnel sees every write. `AdvanceLastSeq` is an `Update` mutator (cmd/lenny-gateway/session_messaging.go:270-281), and the only `UPDATE sessions` statement in the tree is pgstore's updateSQL (pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:420). Writes that bypass the funnel cannot freeze active session age. — EVIDENCE: grep -rln "UPDATE sessions" pkg cmd

CODE-6 item 18.2's `installTurnTap` inside `NewGRPCServer` therefore sees the production runtime, and `turnTapped` is true in production. `SDKWarmInProcessRuntime` embeds `*InProcessRuntime` (pkg/adapter/embedded_sdkwarm.go:22-27), so it inherits `TapOutput`. — EVIDENCE: as cited
FACT: The Postgres lease fallback's `Get` filters `expires_at > now` (pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:163-177). CODE-5's per-sweep `releaseMirror` on a podless `suspended` row therefore stops once the 60 s lease lapses on both lease backends, and the extra mirror writes stay bounded at about 4 per suspension. — EVIDENCE: as cited
FACT: `resolveResumeChunks` returns nil grants when a manifest lookup fails transiently (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_checkpoint.go:69-73). Under CODE-6 item 18.3 that Resume carries no chunks and no count, and the adapter starts at 0. That is correct, because nothing is restored that could hold a turn. The restore-nothing behavior on a lookup failure predates 0092. — EVIDENCE: as cited
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding this round — BECAUSE each new per-message cost is small against the stated budgets: two Redis peeks and one primary read per path-2 message, against about 3,000 req/s at Tier 3 and a Redis budget of about 100k ops/s (spec/17 §17.8; spec/12 §12.4). Idle-suspension claim churn is at most about 11 extra claims/s at 10,000 sessions and a 900 s idle bound, against the 30/s claim profile in the §4.6.1 etcd table. The leader re-check, `sweepSuspended`, and the Sweeper skip are bounded as Settled states. — ALTERNATIVES: filing ReadPrimary-as-`FOR UPDATE` WAL amplification was rejected. It is an IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE example, and the message path already makes a per-event `AdvanceLastSeq` row write, which costs more.

### [non-spec.13.review-security.1]
DECISION: returned no findings under the security lens — BECAUSE every bound the proposal adds or touches is sourced from a trusted component or is not a security bound: session age and the suspended lifetime are gateway-measured Postgres columns, the lease generation scope is persisted, the off-holder held-pod /resume and a failed lease read fail closed, and the adapter turn count only gates idle suspension (maxSessionAge, which the gateway measures, bounds it) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the runtime's ability to lower the count with spurious `response` frames (it harms only its own session, and the runtime is the shared trust domain on a concurrent pod); filing the best-effort budget-key re-arm (strictly better than the pre-proposal 24 h TTL with no re-arm; fail-closed detection is 7(d)); filing tenant-deletion residue of DLQ/inbox keys (filed as RECORDS-1 7(p); the DLQ key had no EXPIRE before this proposal).
FACT: credential leases persist as a JSON blob (`lease` column), so the new `credential.Lease.CoordinationGeneration` survives restarts and cross-replica `ReleaseThrough` with no migration — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:129-146, :419-435
FACT: hold state rejects every adapter RPC outside `coordinatorHoldAllowedMethods` with UNAVAILABLE `coordinator_hold`, which matches CODE-6 item 4's statement for SealSession/UnsealSession — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:52-58, :420-443

### [non-spec.16.fix-G1.1]
MISTAKE: CODE-4 item 8 built a two-`%w` `PreconditionError` in `resumeHeldPod` for every non-`suspended` locked row, so a row the race moved to `awaiting_client_action` (a state `/resume` admits) answered 409 with `currentState` inside `allowedStates`. Deleted it.
DECISION: On `errResumeHeldPodNotSuspended` the held-pod branch of `handleResume` falls back to the `awaiting_client_action` branch (`enterResumePending`); that write's guard miss (CODE-4 item 9) is the only source of the `/resume` 409 — BECAUSE an admitted state then succeeds and every unadmitted one fails with no state enumeration — ALTERNATIVES: keep the wrap plus an `awaiting_client_action` exception (two 409 sources); build the error only for states `session.Validate` rejects (still needs a re-branch); re-read and re-dispatch (extra read, rejected in round 13).
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce a hand-built `PreconditionError` in `resumeHeldPod`, and do not name CODE-6's `errResumeHeldPodReleased` from CODE-4 (S12 must not reference S13). The tree's bare sentinel stays, so `resume_held_pod_test.go` `errors.Is` assertions hold — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_held_pod.go:22, :65; resume_held_pod_test.go:115, :180.

### [non-spec.16.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the held-pod `handleResume` branch answers `errResumeHeldPodNotSuspended` by taking the `awaiting_client_action` branch (`enterResumePending` from `awaiting_client_action`), whose own guard miss (CODE-4 item 9) is the precondition error for every other state; the hand-built `PreconditionError` wrap in `resumeHeldPod` (the sentence moved CODE-4 -> CODE-6 -> CODE-4 in rounds 13-14) is DELETED, and the tree's bare sentinel stays — BECAUSE the wrap built a 409 for every non-`suspended` locked row, including `awaiting_client_action`, which `EndpointResume` admits; reusing the one guarded write makes the admitted state succeed and every unadmitted state 409 from a single source, with no state enumeration — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "when the locked row is `awaiting_client_action`, call enterResumePending" appended to the wrap (an exception clause on a claim that already failed; keeps two 409 sources); building the PreconditionError only for states `session.Validate(EndpointResume)` rejects (same, one step narrower; still needs the awaiting re-branch); a ReadPrimary re-read and re-dispatch (rejected in round 13 as an extra read for a race).
FACT: `resumeHeldPod`'s mutator returns the bare `errResumeHeldPodNotSuspended` today, and `resume_held_pod_test.go` asserts it with `errors.Is`; deleting the wrap leaves those tests valid — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_held_pod.go (resumeHeldPod guard)
WATCHOUT: do not reinstate the two-`%w` PreconditionError wrap in `resumeHeldPod` in either CODE-4 or CODE-6; the 409 for a raced held-pod `/resume` comes from `enterResumePending`'s guard miss. `errResumeHeldPodReleased` (CODE-6 item 6, S13) is checked before this fallback and is untouched.
WATCHOUT: a double race (row leaves `awaiting_client_action` for `suspended` again before the fallback write) still yields a 409 naming `suspended`; that needs a new interrupt-suspension inside one handler call and was judged not worth a loop.

### [non-spec.16.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in non-spec round 16 — BECAUSE the r14-prefix delta only moves the `resumeHeldPod` not-`suspended` PreconditionError wrap from CODE-6 item 6 into CODE-4 item 8 and adds `resume_held_pod.go` to CODE-4 Targets; it adds no store write, Redis call, RPC, watch, or lock. A whole read of CODE-4, CODE-5, and CODE-6 under this lens found nothing new: the turn count rides the existing manifest finalise UPDATE and resume-selection read, and it lives in adapter memory, so it survives a coordinator handoff and a Redis reset. `HeldLen` errors during a Redis outage only defer an interrupt-hold release. The shipped watchdog never releases a held pod (`MaxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` is read only by config defaulting, pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:197-250), so that deferral is no regression — ALTERNATIVES: (1) the unpaced `SuspendIdle` step-3 and `completeResume` commit repeats during a Postgres failover (rejected: Traps **Commit repeat pacing.**, refuted many times); (2) `CREATE INDEX` without CONCURRENTLY in migration 0183, and the CHECK-validating ADD COLUMN in 0182 and 0184 (rejected: no spec or repo migration rule requires online DDL, only 0156 uses CONCURRENTLY, and the platform has no deployments); (3) a restore from a mid-turn periodic checkpoint pinning a pod until maxSessionAge (rejected: accepted in the spec Edge cases bullet **A turn the runtime never answers.**, and pinned by `TestRestoreFromMidTurnCheckpointNotIdleSuspended_spec_4_7_1`); (4) per-replica `DriveResume` lease reads for each `resume_pending` row on each 5 s tick (rejected: the population is transient and bounded by the resume window, and the rate is far inside the ~100k ops/s Redis budget).
FACT: the watchdog is not leader-gated. Every replica runs `Tick` over every tenant and lists each state with its own `List` (pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:588-666), so a per-row call that a new sweep adds runs once per replica per tick unless D2 (the local-binding check) returns first. Check D2 ordering before costing any new per-row call. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:588-666, cmd/lenny-gateway/controlserver.go:221
USEFUL [non-spec.8.review-performance.1]: its Tier-3 idle-checkpoint bound (~11/s, ~1.1 GB/s against the MinIO budget) meant the checkpoint-volume question needed no re-derivation.
USEFUL [non-spec.13.review-performance.1]: its per-message cost math (two Redis peeks plus one primary read per path-2 message) still holds after the delta.

### [non-spec.17.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-4 item 8 held-pod fallback on `errResumeHeldPodNotSuspended` calls `enterResumePending` with the read row's `State` set to `awaiting_client_action` — BECAUSE `enterResumePending` guards on the passed row's state and `resumeHeldPod` returns no row, so a routing pointer to "the branch above" passed the read `suspended` row and the guard missed (409) where the test expects 200 `resume_pending` — ALTERNATIVES: re-read and re-dispatch (extra read, pulls in read-failure table); explicit expected-state parameter (changes every CODE-4 item 9 caller); widened guard (lets ReleaseSuspendedPod 3a, SuspendIdle tail, and BacklogRechecker resume an awaiting_client_action row); r14 PreconditionError wrap (answered 409 for an admitted state).
WATCHOUT: a "take branch X" pointer at a call site whose callee guards on its argument's state is not a specification; name the argument the call receives. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_held_pod.go resumeHeldPod returns only an error.

### [non-spec.17.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-4 item 8 held-pod branch, on errResumeHeldPodNotSuspended, calls enterResumePending with the read row's State set to awaiting_client_action — BECAUSE enterResumePending's source-state guard is the passed row's State (non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 "so that ... enterResumePending guards on suspended"; ResumeSuspended D1-refuses bullet), and resumeHeldPod returns no row (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_held_pod.go resumeHeldPod), so "take the branch above" passed a suspended row whose guard misses on an awaiting_client_action locked row — ALTERNATIVES: re-read with ReadPrimary and re-dispatch (adds a read and drags in the read-failure outcome table); explicit source-state parameter on enterResumePending (changes every caller); widening enterResumePending's guard to every EndpointResume state (would let ReleaseSuspendedPod/BacklogRechecker resume an awaiting_client_action row, which needs a client action); restoring the r14 PreconditionError wrap (r16 removed it because it answered 409 for an admitted state).
WATCHOUT: enterResumePending's expected source state is carried by the State of the row argument, nowhere else. Any new fallback that routes to it after a mutator miss must say which State it passes. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 9; CODE-6 item 6 errResumeHeldPodReleased route works only because the read row is already suspended.

### [non-spec.18.review-mechanism.1]

Item 9 guards only on the source state (plus `!holdsPod` for `suspended`), so a row moved to `awaiting_client_action` reaches `resume_pending`, and any other row misses the guard and gets the precondition error (409). `enterResumePending` takes no delivery lock, so calling it while holding the lock is safe. ### [non-spec.19.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in non-spec round 19 — BECAUSE the r17-prefix delta is one line in CODE-4 item 8 (held-pod `/resume` on `errResumeHeldPodNotSuspended` calls `enterResumePending` with the read row's `State` set to `awaiting_client_action`); it adds at most one guarded Postgres `Update` on a rare race path and no Redis call, RPC, watch, or lock (`enterResumePending` takes no delivery lock). A re-read of CODE-6 items 2, 4 (release, `SuspendIdle`, seal), 7 (routing), 16, 17, and 18 (turn count) under this lens found no new cost: the count rides the existing manifest finalise UPDATE and resume-selection read and lives in adapter memory, so it survives a coordinator handoff and a Redis reset — ALTERNATIVES: (1) an unknown-count restore (partial manifest or NULL `turn_count`) pinning an abandoned session's pod until `maxSessionAge` because every idle `SealSession` is refused (rejected: the owner-staged window **A turn in flight across an unknown-count restore.**, OD-S3); (2) a seal left after an idle `Stale` outcome delaying held messages until the adopter's next idle firing (rejected: SPEC-5ab **Session seal.** / OD-R9-B settled); (3) per-tick `SealSession` re-asks on a session with turns > 0 (rejected in [spec.16.review-performance.1]).

### [non-spec.19.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no findings under the reliability lens in non-spec round 19 — BECAUSE the r17-prefix delta is one sentence (CODE-4 item 8: on `errResumeHeldPodNotSuspended`, call `enterResumePending` with the read row's `State` set to `awaiting_client_action`). The delivery lock and D1 keep the other held-pod writers off the row, so the guarded write admits only an `awaiting_client_action` row (200 `resume_pending`) and answers 409 for anything else. The tier-1 `handleResume` case pins both outcomes. A whole trace of the turn count found no release with a turn in flight outside the SPEC-5ab windows. The trace covered the Checkpoint summary read after `ArchiveTree`, `onSummary` -> `Finalise(partial=false)`, the partial row with a nil count, `Resolve` reading the row it grants, the `LatestFull` and ref fallbacks, and the adapter `Resume`, the only Resume caller, which always runs on a freshly claimed entry — ALTERNATIVES: the `session_state` drain label taking `awaiting_client_action` from the overwritten row (label only, not correctness); per-tick `SealSession` RPCs for a session that holds a turn outstanding forever (performance lens, and the no-retry-time rule was already refuted).
FACT: the adapter `Resume` handler is reached only from `podsession.Binder.Resume` (`resume.go:152`) through `resumeOnPod`'s checkpoint branch (`resume_rebind.go:102`). No coordinator-handoff path sends `Resume` to a live entry, so the item 18.3 reset ("turns = 0 when no chunks") never zeroes the count of a session already running — EVIDENCE: grep of `.Resume(` under pkg/gateway and cmd/lenny-gateway; pkg/adapter/resume.go:44-50 comment mentions a "coordinator-handoff resume" that no caller sends.
FACT: `resumechunks.Resolve` reads the manifest row through `Manifests.Get` before it mints grants, so taking `TurnCount` from "the row whose grants they return" needs no extra read — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/resumechunks/resumechunks.go:120-121.
USEFUL [Standing context, Traps **Commit repeat pacing.**]: it stopped me from refiling the unpaced `completeResume` and `SuspendIdle` step-3 repeats.

### [non-spec.19.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding this round. BECAUSE every bound I traced has a trusted, durable source: active session age accrues in the Postgres store funnel (CODE-1), the retry count and suspended lifetime (`SuspendedAt`) are Postgres columns, D1 fails closed when the lease read fails, and the off-holder held-pod `/resume` fails closed with `TARGET_NOT_READY`. ALTERNATIVES: I considered filing the adapter turn count as a pod self-report that bounds idle suspension, and rejected it. A runtime that never writes `response` keeps its seal refused, but the gateway-measured `maxSessionAge` still bounds that session (SPEC-5ab **Session seal.**; DefaultMaxSessionAgeSeconds=7200 at pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:112). The count also bounds no cross-session or cross-tenant property.
FACT: the interrupt suspension release (`ReleaseSuspendedPod` step 3) has no generation conjunct, but two things keep a stale coordinator safe. The adopter's `CoordinatorFence` makes the stale replica's step-2 checkpoint fail at the adapter, and the row guard (suspended, same `SuspendedAt`, same pod) loses to any adopter write that leaves `suspended`. Only the idle write needs the generation conjunct. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 step 3; settled **Release guard.**
FACT: the existing MCP `lenny/interrupt_session` handler performs no authorization beyond tenant scoping (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:524-557). Proxying it through `postByID` (CODE-6 item 1) therefore drops no control, and it adds the locked-row validation.
FACT: on a non-recycling pool, the idle-suspension release drains the pod (SPEC-5b step 2 "any other pod drains"), so the exclusive-pod rule in spec/05:426 ("terminates when the session ends") still holds. The tier-9 `TestResumedSessionAuthenticatesWithFreshLease` asserts that the old proxy token is rejected after the idle release (non-spec-changes.md Tier 9 bullet).

### [spec.17.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: an unaddressed `heartbeat_ack` is attributed to no session for the seal (SPEC-5ab item 2); it still answers liveness — BECAUSE the adapter writes each heartbeat on one stream naming one session, so a bare ack says nothing about a streamless co-tenant, and attributing it to every session let that co-tenant pass the no-ack refusal and be sealed mid-work — ALTERNATIVES: attribute to sessions heartbeated since the last ack (new per-entry state, closes nothing more); attribute only on a single-slot pod (compatibility path); make `sessionId` Required=Yes on the ack (forces SDK, tier-1, tier-3 churn; a bare ack is harmless once it attributes nothing).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce "attributed to every session on the pod" for bare acks; a runtime that acks bare is refused every seal and `maxSessionAge` bounds it, which is the intended fail-closed outcome. CODE-6 item 18 `observeRuntimeFrame` and `TestActivityClockStampsEveryFrame_spec_4_7_1` now assert a bare ack sets nothing — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:318-323 passes an unaddressed ack to every stream's liveness monitor, which is a separate behavior.
FACT: the SPEC-5ab activity bullet for runtime frames no longer cites §28.5.3 **Addressing.** (that rule resolves only against the delivering stream); it states the `deliverToSession` resolution directly: `sessionId` names the session, or no `sessionId` on a pod with at most one slot — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:376-395.
FACT: SPEC-5ab item 4 now carries two edits to the §28.5.1 CH-ATTACH **Timing.** bullet: the seal deadline sentence and the stream-lifetime pointer to §6.2 **Idle suspension.** for the earlier opening — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:241-242.
