### [spec.17.fix-G2.1]
MISTAKE: an earlier round staged in SPEC-5ab item 2 "While a sealed session is `running`, the next idle firing ... seals it again and either completes the suspension ... or lifts the seal." That contradicted the not-sealed answer (a seal stays in place) and SPEC-5f's write-nothing-else rule for a not-sealed answer. A sealed session with continuing activity reaches neither outcome. The sentence is deleted, and the bounds that remain are "a later attempt lifts it or the entry is removed" plus `maxSessionAge`.
WATCHOUT: do not make the deleted sentence true by adding UnsealSession on a not-sealed answer. That reopens the single-lifter rule (only the attempt that placed the seal lifts it, and only on checkpoint failure or a same-generation same-pod write miss). EVIDENCE: spec-changes SPEC-5ab item 2, paragraph "The gateway calls `SealSession` only at the start of an idle suspension attempt".
FACT: the edge-case bullets **Work begun without a frame just before the seal.** and **An adapter that refuses the seal or does not answer, ...** now only point at SPEC-5ab, SPEC-5ac, SPEC-5f, and SPEC-5i. They carry no rule text of their own. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Edge cases section.

### [spec.17.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: a `heartbeat_ack` naming no session is attributed to NO session (SPEC-5ab item 2), and non-spec 418/669 drop the "every entry" branch — BECAUSE heartbeats are per-`Attach`-stream (pkg/adapter/heartbeat.go `startHeartbeat`), so only a session-named ack proves the runtime was asked about that session; a streamless co-tenant then fails the no-ack test and SPEC-5f opens its stream — ALTERNATIVES: "attributed to every session heartbeated since the last ack" (new adapter state, no gain since the adapter now always names the session); single-slot exception mirroring Addressing (a compat path for bare-acking runtimes; none exist in the wild); SPEC-5ac `sessionId` Required = Yes (forces SDK unheld-session answer and tier-3 schema case to change; bare ack stays harmless once it attributes nothing).
FACT: the tree fans every unaddressed protocol frame (heartbeat_ack) to every stream's heartbeat monitor for LIVENESS (pkg/adapter/attach.go `sessionScopedFrameTypes` comment); liveness and seal attribution are separate and SPEC-5ac keeps "Any heartbeat_ack answers the liveness probe". EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:300-323
WATCHOUT: the §28.5.3 **Addressing.** rule resolves only against the delivering Attach stream; do not cite it for the stream-independent activity tap. The unaddressed-frame resolution (≤1 slot resolves, >1 slot rejects) is what `deliverToSession` implements. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:944-954; pkg/adapter/attach.go:376

### [spec.17.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in the spec-round-17 staging (redesign 13: `busy` on `heartbeat_ack`, OD-S1 cap exemption, resume admission) — BECAUSE the new costs are small at Tier 3/4: a busy or `type: mcp` session costs one `SealSession` RPC plus one `last_agent_activity_at` write per idle window (10,000/900 s ≈ 11/s at Tier 3, ≈ 111/s at Tier 4), and SPEC-5f re-arms the clock one window out on every outcome, so nothing re-asks on each 5 s tick. Per-session heartbeats stay inside the pod. The admission check runs only on exits from `suspended`, and with every cap at its default of 0 it issues no query. Under a §12.3 primary failover the message hold path already fails at its primary re-read (**Enqueue racing a terminal transition.**), so the primary-only count queries add no new outage — ALTERNATIVES: (1) the leader re-check's per-pass cost over an uncapped podless population (rejected for this loop: the existing per-replica watchdog and Sweeper full scans dominate it, per [non-spec.11.review-performance.1]); (2) held-pod `suspended` sessions escaping the per-user cap (rejected: the owner decided OD-S1 "with or without its pod", summary.md:43).
CORRECTS [Standing context **Leader capacity.**]: the podless `suspended` population is no longer bounded by the concurrent-session caps. SPEC-6a exempts `suspended` from every cap, and the caps default to 0 anyway (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:1111 "the cap defaults to 0, disabled"). The bound is now creation rate × time to expiry: §16.5 Tier 3 sustains 200 creations/s (spec/16_observability.md:596), and the time to expiry is `maxSuspendedSessionSeconds` (604800 s) plus one idle window. About 84 times more abandoned sessions stay non-terminal than under the 7200 s idle termination.
CORRECTS [Standing context **Refuted: the 7-day suspended population.**]: its premise "bounded by the caps" no longer holds after OD-S1. The leader pass is still small next to the pre-existing full scans, but the Sweeper's per-row `leases.Get` over podless rows (every replica, every 15 s) now scales with the 7-day population.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-7 item 1 says the new count predicate `state NOT IN (terminal…, 'suspended')` "implies the migration-0102 partial-index predicate, so no migration is added". The implication is true, but 0102 indexes only `(tenant_id)` WHERE non-terminal (migrations/0102_sessions_active_by_tenant_index.up.sql:17-19), and `state` is not an index column. So every creation and resume COUNT must fetch from the heap every `suspended` row of the tenant (or of all tenants for the global cap) to exclude it, and that population is now 7-day and uncapped. What is true instead: the count needs a partial index whose predicate also excludes `suspended`, or one that includes `state`. Owner: the non-spec performance lens.
OPEN: under OD-S1 "with or without its pod", a user at the per-user cap can hold more pods by repeatedly creating a session and interrupting it, each pod held up to `maxSuspendedPodHoldSeconds` (900 s) uncounted. Only the creation rate limit and the namespace ResourceQuota bound this. A human should confirm the owner intended the held-pod half of the exemption.
FACT: every `CountActiveSessions*` query runs on the primary pool (`s.pool`), not the read pool — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:862, :885, :909, :931
USEFUL [non-spec.11.review-performance.1]: its WATCHOUT about pre-existing full scans settled which side of the bar the uncapped-population leader cost falls on.

### [spec.17.review-reliability.1]
- FACT: Concurrent-session caps are Postgres COUNT queries (sessionstore CountActiveSessions*, pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:1027-1044), not a Redis counter, so excluding `suspended` needs no counter reconciliation on Redis failover. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:1027
- FACT: The orphan-claim GC predicate keys on `pod_assignment` (spec/04_system-components.md:517), so a crash between the suspension release's clearing write and the pod release leaves a claim the GC drains; this is the reclaimer for the reversed clear-then-release order of SPEC-5b step 2 and SPEC-5f. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517
- FACT: Watchdog ticks every 5 s (pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:166). The idle path's 60 s checkpoint-failure gate exists only in CODE-6 item 4 (`SuspendIdle`, non-spec:316); SPEC-5f states no retry pacing after a failed idle checkpoint, while §6.2 release step 3 (spec/06:230) states 60 s for the interrupt release. Filed this round.
- WATCHOUT: `suspended → expired` by the suspended lifetime is evaluated by every replica's `sweepSuspended` with a guarded write (non-spec CODE-6 item 2.1), so a podless row with no lease still expires. Do not file "no coordinator expires a podless suspended session". — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 2
- UNVERIFIED: On a `maxConcurrentSessions > 1` pod, a crash between the clearing write and the slot release leaves the session's adapter slot and Redis slot occupancy with no referencing row while other sessions keep the pod referenced, so the orphan-claim GC never drains it. Whether the §10.1.4 hold-state timeout reclaims it is unclear (hold is pod-scoped). Not filed; a Kubernetes or mechanism reviewer could check.

### [spec.17.review-security.1]
FACT: The concurrent-session counts the resume admission check reuses are Postgres COUNTs over the sessions table, so they need no Redis durable fallback. Per-tenant uses `CountActiveSessions`, and per-user, per-runtime, and global use `CountActive*`. A counter error fails closed with 500. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/quota.go:41, :185-218
FACT: The §11.1 control table lists a per-team concurrency scope, but no limit surface exists for it in code, so SPEC-6a can omit it from the resume check without leaving a gate open. — EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:8; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/quota.go:168-172
FACT: `busy` on `heartbeat_ack` is a runtime self-report, but it bounds no security property. It only defers idle suspension, and `maxSessionAge` is gateway-measured active time that accrues while `running`, so it still bounds the session. Do not file it under the pod-self-report bar.
FACT: The SPEC-1g3 renewal check fails closed on a store error. Per non-spec CODE-4 item 23, a `Lookup` error is retried rather than treated as a released lease, and it never triggers a renewal of a released lease.
OPEN: OD-S9 (an open owner decision, not filed here): the failure edge out of a held-pod `suspended` session can be triggered by the session's own runtime crashing during an interrupt hold. Suspended sessions are exempt from the caps, so the number of sessions that can take this unchecked re-entry is bounded only by the hold window and pool capacity, not by the in-flight concurrency that SPEC-6a **Concurrent admissions.** suggests. The owner may want that stated when deciding OD-S9.
DECISION: no security findings on the staged spec edits — BECAUSE the held-pod cap exemption is owner decision OD-S1 (summary item 27, "with or without its pod"), every suspended→running/resume_pending write in SPEC-5i/SPEC-6a carries the admission check, and no edit adds pod RBAC, egress, or a webhook dependency — ALTERNATIVES: filing the failure-edge bypass was rejected as an open decision's framing.

### [spec.18.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: an elicitation wait is `running` and the idle clock runs there; the wait blocks idle suspension only through the runtime's `busy` report (SPEC-5p replaced, SPEC-5e `input_required` row loses its elicitation sentence) — BECAUSE only `lenny/request_input` enters `input_required`, and the staged `busy` rule already counts a tool call such as `lenny/request_elicitation` as work — ALTERNATIVES: a pending-elicitation skip in SPEC-5f/`sweepIdle` (parallel predicate to `busy`); routing elicitation through `input_required` or a new state (contradicts §9.2's independence of the two mechanisms); deleting SPEC-5p (leaves the tree's §9.2 reason "a wait on an absent client is the condition the bound exists to reclaim", which `busy` falsifies).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce "Not evaluated" for elicitation waits in SPEC-5e, SPEC-5p, or the watchdog; `idleClockRunningStates` stays `{running}` with no elicitation handling. A runtime that never reports `busy` is sealed mid-elicitation by design (SPEC-5ac). — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:192 (only entry to input_required), spec/09_mcp-integration.md:110, pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go:22
FACT: the §9.2 **Idle clock:** item is at spec/09_mcp-integration.md:102; the `heartbeat_ack` label is spec/28_communication-channels.md:868 under heading 28.5.3 (anchor #2853-intra-pod). — EVIDENCE: those lines

### [spec.18.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5t's §27.6 replacements point at §6.2 **Idle suspension.** ("the gateway attempts the idle suspension that ... states") and the browser-close replacement keeps the live verb "fires" — BECAUSE SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** is the one home of idle outcomes, including the refusals (type: mcp, heartbeats disabled, acks without sessionId, no ack) where the session stays `running` and no pod is released — ALTERNATIVES: listing the refusal cases in §27.6 (second statement of SPEC-5f, drifts); a playground fallback that force-releases an unsealable session (new tier-dependent mechanism, owner's call).
OPEN: live §27.6 "Playground-initiated sessions MUST NOT remain idle for longer than `playground.maxIdleTimeSeconds`" cannot hold for a runtime whose seals the adapter refuses (bare `heartbeat_ack` such as the §15.4.4 sample, or `type: mcp`); such a playground session is bounded by the hard duration cap (maxSessionAge stamp). Whether that guarantee needs a playground fallback or a reworded MUST is a human decision. EVIDENCE: spec/27_web-playground.md:204; spec-changes.md SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**; spec-changes.md Edge cases bullet "A runtime whose `busy` the adapter cannot observe."
WATCHOUT: summary.md OD-S7 entry still says "Under this proposal (SPEC-5t and CODE-6) the idle bound suspends the session and releases its pod instead"; the whole OD-S7 entry is stale (answered (b) by the caller directive) and its removal is a separate finding, so it was not edited here.

Not filed by this lens because whether an open decision is applied is outside the finding bar; the staged OD-S8 (a) mechanism works end to end (refusal, stream open, heartbeats, decision one window later).
DECISION: returned no findings — BECAUSE every traced flow (idle seal/checkpoint/write/release/backlog, held delivery, cap admission on each resume path, lease retention across idle suspension) is consistent across SPEC-5ab, 5ac, 5e, 5f, 5i, 6a and the surrounding spec — ALTERNATIVES: filing the send_message "tool error" against §7.2 "All lenny/send_message calls return a deliveryReceipt" (rejected: the tree and spec already return tool errors for CROSS_TENANT, interceptor, and cooldown refusals, so the contradiction predates this proposal).

### [spec.21.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in the spec-round-21 staging — BECAUSE the only top-tier cost that grows is the gateway leader's backlog re-check over the uncapped 7-day podless `suspended` population (Tier 3: ~1.2M rows per 1% of 200 creations/s that end idle; one Redis `HeldLen` per row per 300 s), and the shipped watchdog and Sweeper already list every session row, terminal rows included, on every replica each tick, so the leader pass adds no new O(rows) scan; the SealSession RPC plus one `last_agent_activity_at` write per idle window (~11/s Tier 3) and in-pod per-session heartbeats are negligible; under a Postgres failover, a Redis reset, or a coordinator handoff nothing staged degrades below the shipped design (podless rows hold no lease; held messages share the DLQ's pre-existing Redis-loss exposure) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the leader pass as a single-leader bottleneck (rejected, same ground as [spec.17.review-performance.1] and [non-spec.11.review-performance.1]: dominated by pre-existing full scans, and the spec does not forbid pipelining).
USEFUL [spec.17.review-performance.1]: its CORRECTS of **Leader capacity.** and the population math meant this round did not re-derive the uncapped bound; its DEFERRED on the 0102 index is still the live performance gap.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: compounding the [spec.17.review-performance.1] 0102-index DEFERRED: with a cap enabled and full, CODE-7 item 5 has `BacklogRechecker.Pass` call `admitResume` (up to four COUNTs on the primary) for every podless row holding a message on every pass, and each COUNT heap-fetches every `suspended` row of the tenant (all tenants for the global cap) because 0102 indexes only `(tenant_id)` over non-terminal rows. The pass cost is then (held rows) x (suspended rows) per 300 s. A partial index excluding `suspended` (or including `state`) in CODE-7 fixes both. Owner: non-spec performance lens.
FACT: the spec staging carries no turn count, count carriage, or unknown-count state; "turn" survives only in SPEC-5e's direct-mode wording and the SPEC-5ac `busy` description — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5e, SPEC-5ac item 2.

SPEC-5ac item 1 still says the adapter writes `heartbeat` "for each session whose `Attach` stream is open", and summary.md:32 and :149 still carry OD-S8 as open with the gateway-OpenStream workaround, while the caller's delegated OD-S8 (b) answer says heartbeats go to every live registry entry from session_start. ### [spec.21.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no findings under the reliability lens in spec round 21 — BECAUSE each crash/restart path the staging adds closes: a crash after the idle suspend write and before the release leaves a pod no `pod_assignment` names, which orphan-GC predicate 1 drains after `claimOrphanTimeout` (spec/04_system-components.md:517), and the lost once-evaluation is covered by the leader re-check (SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.**); a message refused by the seal and re-routed is enqueued before its `resume_pending` write, and the releasing replica evaluates the backlog only after its suspend write, so no ordering strands it; a podless row moved to `resume_pending` is adopted through SPEC-2a after the lease lapses — ALTERNATIVES: (1) a seal whose `SealSession` answer is lost (5 s deadline) or whose attempt went stale stays in place, and "answers not sealed" keeps it, so messages are held until a later full idle window or `maxSessionAge`. Rejected: SPEC-5ab **Session seal.** states this outcome and the bound, and the caller directive settles seal lifting there. (2) Checkpoint-failure re-seal on every watchdog tick, which would stampede the object store. Rejected: the spec writes nothing on a checkpoint failure, and non-spec CODE-6 item 4 gates retries at 60 s, so any remedy would be code. (3) The unstaged OD-S7 (b) and OD-S8 (b) answers. Rejected: summary.md records both as open decisions ("Reported answer, not staged"), and spec.21 security, single-source, and citations declined them for the same reason.
WATCHOUT: the release paths in SPEC-5b step 2 and SPEC-5f clear `pod_assignment` before they release. Orphan GC is therefore the only reclaimer when the releasing replica dies in between. A slot-pool pod never meets predicate 1, because other sessions reference it, so the sealed slot stays until the pod ends. This is the slot-pool orphan GC entry already in **Not filed, do not refile.** — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517
USEFUL [spec.21.review-kubernetes.1]: its note that predicate 1 counts only non-terminal rows naming the pod confirmed the reclaimer for the crash-between-write-and-release window.

### [spec.21.review-security.1]
FACT: The concurrent-session caps are counted from Postgres session rows (`CountActiveSessions*`), and a count error already fails closed with 500 on creation, so the SPEC-6a resume check sources its bound from a trusted durable store and needs no Redis fallback — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/quota.go:40-56, :169-190
FACT: §11.5 caches the full response (status and body) for 24 h per `(tenant_id, idempotency_key)`, so SPEC-6a's "retry a refused resume with a new key" sentence is accurate — EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md §11.5 **Mechanism.**
FACT: The per-team concurrency scope in the §11.1 control table has no admin-settable limit in code, so SPEC-6a omitting it is not a cap bypass — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/quota.go:161-166 (requireConcurrencyLimits doc comment)
FACT: §12.8 erasure lets in-flight sessions run to completion during `DeleteByUser`, so a DLQ enqueue between step 10 and the `SessionStore` deletion is the same window every other store already has. It is not a 0092 regression, and 0092 adds the DLQ and inbox to the erasure scope for the first time — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:807 (Processing restriction), :849-875
DECISION: Filed no security findings this round — BECAUSE the `busy` self-report and the adapter's idle answer only decide whether a pod is released. `maxSessionAge` (active time, measured by the gateway) still bounds the session, the seal is enforced by the platform adapter rather than the runtime, and every `suspended` → `running`/`resume_pending` write is cap-checked except the OD-S9 failure edge, which is already settled — ALTERNATIVES: filing OD-S7/OD-S8 as unstaged directive answers. Rejected because the summary records both as open decisions with "Reported answer, not staged", and this lens does not file on open decisions.
UNVERIFIED: OD-S8 (b), where the adapter heartbeats every registry entry and the busy_unobservable refusal and gateway stream-opening are deleted, is still unstaged. SPEC-5f still opens `Attach` on a refusal, SPEC-5ac item 1 still limits heartbeats to open streams, and Edge cases line 28 still describes the stream workaround. OD-S7 (b), which has the §27.6 hard cap count active time, is also unstaged. The fixer or the open-decisions pass should apply them. A directive-conformance lens owns this, not security.

### [spec.23.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in the spec-round-23 staging — BECAUSE the round-22 delta (idle expiry kept for `type: mcp` and service-mode sessions, per-entry heartbeats independent of `Attach`, the SPEC-6a single refusal home) adds no store write class: heartbeats are in-pod CH-MSGSOCK frames, idle expiry is the shipped `expired` write, and resume admission adds only cap COUNTs already covered by the 0184 index entry — ALTERNATIVES: (1) per-tick retry of a failed idle-suspension checkpoint (SPEC-5f states no backoff), rejected because the spec does not forbid one and CODE-6 item 4 already skips a session whose last checkpoint failure is under 60 s old; (2) etcd claim churn from idle-resume cycles, rejected because the §16.5 workload model (avgSessionDurationSeconds 333 s, ~30 claims/s at Tier 3) puts the 900 s idle window beyond the mean session, so the added claim rate is small against the ~800 writes/s estimate; (3) the single-leader backlog pass, barred by Standing context **Leader capacity.** and [spec.21.review-performance.1].
FACT: the §4.6.1 etcd estimate derives the Tier 3 claim rate from Little's Law over `capacityPlanning.avgSessionDurationSeconds` = 333 s — EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:609,616; spec/04_system-components.md:475
FACT: during a Postgres primary failover the watchdog can still list rows from the read pool, so an idle-due session can reach `SuspendIdle`; its checkpoint intent-row INSERT on the primary fails, which unseals and stops, and the CODE-6 item 4 60 s retry record bounds re-attempts — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 **`SuspendIdle`** opening and step 2
USEFUL [spec.21.review-performance.1]: its tier math and rejected alternatives covered the leader pass and SealSession rates, so this round checked only the round-22 delta.

### [spec.23.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The heartbeat-hung refusal of a later `Attach` is `DeadlineExceeded` in CODE-6 item 18, so it falls under the landed §28.5.1 **Degradation.** stream-failure rule ("ends a session's stream with `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED` when the runtime misses its heartbeat ack"); SPEC-5ac item 7 does not need to name the status. — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:256-258; non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 18 ("ends the stream with `DeadlineExceeded`")
FACT: Checkpoint-failure retry pacing for both the idle attempt and the interrupt release is the 60 s per-replica retry record in CODE-6 item 4 step 1 and `SuspendIdle`; the spec's "writes nothing and releases nothing" leaves the clock fired, so this pacing is what keeps a checkpoint-store outage from re-checkpointing every tick. It lives in code, so it is not a spec finding. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 steps 1-2
FACT: A `HeldLen` error skips the release on that tick (`ResumeBacklog` returns true) and the leader pass logs and goes on. A Redis outage therefore resumes nothing in bulk. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 2 ("On a `HeldLen` error it logs and returns true, so the row gets no release this tick"), item 16
DECISION: no findings in round 23 under the reliability lens — BECAUSE I traced each restart, failover, and redelivery path the staged spec adds (driver entry and commit, resuming watchdog, generation-scoped lease release, idle seal, write, and release, release-time migration, backlog resume at the coordinator, releaser, and leader, podless terminate, and admission refusal). Each one has a guard or a reclaimer, or matches an accepted Settled/Trap entry. — ALTERNATIVES: filing the orphaned-seal stall after a takeover (a client attach resets the gateway clock, which the SPEC-5e client-interaction bullet still lists, so the stall lasts one idle window after the last attach) was rejected because that recovery is only slower and SPEC-5ab already bounds it

### [spec.23.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security findings in spec round 23 — BECAUSE every bound the staging touches is sourced from a trusted durable store or a gateway measurement: the concurrent caps are Postgres COUNTs (SPEC-6a), the suspended lifetime and maxSessionAge accrue from session-row timestamps, budget-key expiry fails closed (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:408-414), and `busy` or the adapter's idle answer decides only pod retention under the gateway-measured maxSessionAge — ALTERNATIVES: filing the unchecked failure edge out of `suspended` as a cap bypass by a crashing tenant runtime was rejected, because OD-S9 (Decision 27) settles it and SPEC-6a **Concurrent admissions.** states the overshoot.
FACT: Renewal after a pod release fails closed on a store error. A `LeaseStore.Lookup` error is retried and mints nothing, and only a confirmed missing record returns `ErrLeaseReleased`, so SPEC-1g3 needs no read-failure clause — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 23.2 (`pgstore` reports missing only on `pgx.ErrNoRows`).
FACT: §6.2 graceful release step 2 currently says "releases the pod back to the pool" (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:229). SPEC-5b narrows it to the pool's session-end disposition, so a non-recycling pod drains. That tightens one-session-only reuse; it does not regress it.
FACT: The `PreMessageDelivery` phase is defined as firing "before the gateway delivers" an inter-session message (spec/04_system-components.md:1148). Running it at acceptance, before the DLQ hold, satisfies it for messages held to a `suspended` target.
WATCHOUT: `sessionPolicy.maxSessionRetries` deletion leaves §7.3 "bounded by deployer caps" (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:386) and §11.2 "deployer-capped" with no named cap in spec. The code cap is `--retry-max-retries`. This is below the security bar (a resource budget whose deletion removes no reader); do not refile under security.
USEFUL [spec.21.review-security.1]: its quota.go fail-closed and §11.5 cached-refusal facts still hold and saved re-deriving them.
